=== NOTES 2:159 | Regional Organisations – Saarc, Bimstec, Asean, Eu, African Union, Sco, Gcc, Iora, Bbin
## SUB 0 | European Union and Council of Europe
[159:0:0] # Evolution
[159:0:1] TABLE head: Year | Step
  [159:0:1.0] 1951 | Treaty of Paris – European Coal and Steel Community (6 states)
  [159:0:1.1] 1957 | Treaty of Rome – EEC (forerunner of the EU) and Euratom
  [159:0:1.2] 1986 | Single European Act
  [159:0:1.3] 1992 | Maastricht Treaty (signed 7 Feb 1992, in force 1993) – created the EU
  [159:0:1.4] 1999 | Euro launched (1 January 1999) only as a unit of account; notes and coins in circulation from 1 January 2002
  [159:0:1.5] 2007 | Bulgaria and Romania join – 27 members
  [159:0:1.6] 2009 | Lisbon Treaty in force (Article 50 exit clause)
  [159:0:1.7] 2013 | Croatia joins (1 July 2013) – latest member; 28
  [159:0:1.8] 2020 | UK leaves, 31 January 2020 – back to 27
[159:0:2] - The EU grew out of the **European Community**; EU citizens hold EU citizenship in addition to national citizenship; **Switzerland is not** a member.
[159:0:3] - The EU has its **own flag, anthem and currency** but **no Constitution** (the 2004 Constitutional Treaty failed); its combined armed forces are the second largest in the world (NCERT).
[159:0:4] - The **Preamble of the proposed EU Constitution** was drafted by **Valéry Giscard d'Estaing**, chairman of the European Convention (2002–03).
[159:0:5] # Brexit
[159:0:6] - Referendum **23 June 2016** (51.9% Leave) → David **Cameron** resigned; **Theresa May** (the Home Secretary) became Conservative leader and PM – the Conservatives stayed in power (the opposition did **not** come to power).
[159:0:7] - Exit under **Article 50 TEU**; UK left **31 January 2020**.
[159:0:8] - **Windsor Framework** (February 2023) – Northern Ireland, UK and EU.
[159:0:9] # Other EU facts
[159:0:10] - **Greek referendum (July 2015)** – whether to accept the **bailout conditions** of the EU and IMF (not on leaving the EU).
[159:0:11] - **Ukraine and Moldova** granted candidate status – European Council, Brussels, **23 June 2022**.
[159:0:12] - **Roberta Metsola** (Malta) – elected **President of the European Parliament**, January 2022.
[159:0:13] - **Digital Single Market Strategy** – EU, adopted **6 May 2015**. **Stability and Growth Pact** – limits budget deficits.
[159:0:14] - **India-EU FTA (2026):** EU commitments in **144 services subsectors**; social security agreements with all EU members within 5 years; post-study work framework for Indian students.
[159:0:15] # Council of Europe
[159:0:16] - Founded **1949**, HQ **Strasbourg**; upholds **human rights, democracy and rule of law** in Europe (European Convention on Human Rights) – **not** an EU body.
[159:0:17] - **47 members** until **Russia was expelled (March 2022)** → now **46**.
[159:0:18] > Exam angle: Maastricht created the EU; Croatia latest member; Brexit = Article 50; Euro not in circulation in 1999.
[159:0:19] ~~ Sources: OnlyIAS NCERT Post-Independence & World History Part 35 p.21 (src/indian-history/onlyias-ncert-postindep-world-history-35.txt); PMF IAS International Relations p.105-107; app notes 2:159 sub 4
## SUB 1 | SAARC and BBIN
[159:1:0] # Basics
[159:1:1] - **Charter signed at Dhaka, 8 December 1985** (1st Summit) – SAARC founded **1985**, not 1984 or 1986.
[159:1:2] - **Secretariat: Kathmandu** (Nepal) – not Dhaka (Charter venue) or New Delhi.
[159:1:3] - **Seven founders:** India, Pakistan, Bangladesh, Sri Lanka, Nepal, Maldives, Bhutan. **Afghanistan** joined at the **14th Summit, April 2007** → **8 members** (not a founder).
[159:1:4] - **Members (8):** Afghanistan, Bangladesh, **Bhutan**, India, Maldives, Nepal, Pakistan, Sri Lanka.
[159:1:5] - **Not members:** **Myanmar** (an observer), China (observer), Tibet (not a state), Malaysia, Iran. SAARC includes every land neighbour of India **except China and Myanmar**.
[159:1:6] - **Observers (9):** EU, Australia, China, Iran, Japan, Republic of Korea, Mauritius, Myanmar, USA – China is **not** the only observer; **Russia is not** an observer.
[159:1:7] # Secretariat and Secretary General
[159:1:8] - The **Secretary General** heads the Secretariat; appointed by the **Council of Ministers** from member states in **alphabetical rotation** for a **three-year** term; assisted by **eight directors** on deputation from member states (CDS 2014: all three statements correct).
[159:1:9] - **31st SAARC Council of Ministers** (February 2009) – **Colombo, Sri Lanka**.
[159:1:10] # Summits
[159:1:11] TABLE head: Summit | Venue / date
  [159:1:11.0] 1st | Dhaka, December 1985 (Charter)
  [159:1:11.1] 12th | Islamabad, January 2004 – SAFTA signed
  [159:1:11.2] 14th | New Delhi, April 2007 – Afghanistan admitted
  [159:1:11.3] 16th | Thimphu, 28–29 April 2010
  [159:1:11.4] 18th (last) | Kathmandu, November 2014
  [159:1:11.5] 19th | Islamabad, 2016 – cancelled after the Uri attack
[159:1:12] # Institutions and India's initiatives
[159:1:13] - **SAPTA** 1995 → **SAFTA:** signed **2004**, in force **1 January 2006**; zero duty on eligible goods meeting Rules of Origin (DRI busted an areca-nut origin fraud, Aug 2026).
[159:1:14] - **South Asian University** – **New Delhi** (2010). **SAARC Development Fund** – **Thimphu**. SAARC Disaster Management Centre – India.
[159:1:15] - India's proposals: **South Asia Satellite (2017)**, **SAARC COVID-19 Emergency Fund (2020)**. SAARC Visa Exemption Scheme (1992) cancelled for Pakistanis after **Pahalgam (2025)**.
[159:1:16] - Not all South Asian states are democracies (e.g., Taliban-ruled Afghanistan since 2021) – the wrong statement in CDS 2011.
[159:1:17] - Jun 2026: SAARC and BIMSTEC both constrained by India's tensions with Islamabad and Dhaka.
[159:1:18] # BBIN Motor Vehicles Agreement
[159:1:19] - **Bangladesh, Bhutan, India, Nepal**; signed at **Thimphu, 2015**; **Bhutan has not ratified** it.
[159:1:20] > Exam angle: Charter **Dhaka** vs Secretariat **Kathmandu**; "not a member" → **Myanmar** (asked 5 times); 8 members.
[159:1:21] ~~ Sources: PMF IAS International Relations p.108-111 (src/international-relations/pmf-international-relations.txt); OnlyIAS NCERT Post-Independence & World History Part 35 p.26, p.29 (src/indian-history/onlyias-ncert-postindep-world-history-35.txt); app notes 2:159 sub 2
## SUB 2 | ASEAN, East Asia Summit and RCEP
[159:2:0] # ASEAN
[159:2:1] - Founded by the **Bangkok Declaration, 8 August 1967** – five founders: Indonesia, Malaysia, Philippines, Singapore, Thailand. **Secretariat: Jakarta**.
[159:2:2] - **11 members** with **Timor-Leste (2025)**: Brunei, Cambodia, Indonesia, Laos, Malaysia, Myanmar, Philippines, Singapore, Thailand, Vietnam, Timor-Leste.
[159:2:3] - **Not members:** **China**, **India**, **South Korea**, Japan (dialogue partners). **Myanmar** joined ASEAN and BIMSTEC in **1997**; it is the **only ASEAN country with a land border with India**.
[159:2:4] - **Timor-Leste (East Timor):** a **Portuguese** colony until 1975, then occupied by Indonesia (1975–99) and run by the UN (1999–2002); independent **20 May 2002**.
[159:2:5] - Geostrategic importance of Southeast Asia: its **location between the Pacific and Indian Oceans** and its maritime character (UPSC 2011).
[159:2:6] # India and ASEAN
[159:2:7] TABLE head: Status | Year
  [159:2:7.0] Sectoral dialogue partner | 1992
  [159:2:7.1] Full dialogue partner | 1996
  [159:2:7.2] Summit-level partner | 2002
  [159:2:7.3] Comprehensive Strategic Partnership | 2022
[159:2:8] - **ASEAN Regional Forum** – **1994**. **AIFTA (goods)** signed 2009, in force **2010**; India sought early completion of the **AITIGA review** (32nd ARF, Kuala Lumpur, July 2025).
[159:2:9] # Summits and meetings
[159:2:10] TABLE head: Meeting | Venue
  [159:2:10.0] 9th ASEAN-Mekong Basin Development Cooperation (AMBDC) Ministerial, August 2007 | Manila
  [159:2:10.1] 15th ASEAN Summit (2009) | Cha-am / Hua Hin, Thailand
  [159:2:10.2] 14th ASEAN-India Summit (8 Sep 2016) | Vientiane, Laos
  [159:2:10.3] 31st ASEAN Summit (Nov 2017) | Manila (Philippines chair)
  [159:2:10.4] 21st ASEAN-India Summit (Oct 2024) | Vientiane again
[159:2:11] # East Asia Summit
[159:2:12] - **First EAS: 14 December 2005, Kuala Lumpur**; 16 countries (10 ASEAN + India, Japan, China, South Korea, Australia, NZ).
[159:2:13] - Now **19**: 11 ASEAN + India, China, Japan, US, Russia, Australia, NZ, South Korea. India is a member of the **EAS**, but **not** of ASEAN or **APEC** (UPSC 2015). **20th EAS (Kuala Lumpur, 2025)** – Kuala Lumpur Declaration.
[159:2:14] # RCEP
[159:2:15] - **ASEAN-led** FTA: ten ASEAN + Australia, China, Japan, NZ, Korea; signed **15 November 2020**; about **30% of world population**; **India stayed out (November 2019)**.
[159:2:16] - Seen as a counter to the US-led **Trans-Pacific Partnership**; often described as China-backed though it is ASEAN-centred.
[159:2:17] # Mekong Ganga Cooperation
[159:2:18] - Launched **10 November 2000, Vientiane**; **6 members**: India, Cambodia, Laos, Myanmar, Thailand, Vietnam; areas: **tourism, culture, education, transport and communications** (no defence).
[159:2:19] > Exam angle: "not an ASEAN member" → China / South Korea / India (asked 3 times); RCEP belongs to ASEAN.
[159:2:20] ~~ Sources: PMF IAS International Relations p.93-94 (src/international-relations/pmf-international-relations.txt); OnlyIAS NCERT Post-Independence & World History Part 35 p.22 (src/indian-history/onlyias-ncert-postindep-world-history-35.txt); asean.org (9th AMBDC Ministerial Meeting, Manila); app notes 2:159 sub 5-6
## SUB 3 | BIMSTEC
[159:3:0] # Name, members, Secretariat
[159:3:1] - **Bay of Bengal Initiative for Multi-Sectoral Technical and Economic Cooperation** – set up by the **Bangkok Declaration, 1997** as **BIST-EC** (Bangladesh, India, Sri Lanka, Thailand); Myanmar joined 1997; **Nepal and Bhutan 2004**.
[159:3:2] - **7 members:** Bangladesh, Bhutan, India, Myanmar, Nepal, Sri Lanka, Thailand – **Pakistan is not** a member. **Secretariat: Dhaka**.
[159:3:3] - A **bridge between South Asia (SAARC) and Southeast Asia (ASEAN)**. It was **never** called the "Bangkok Agreement" (that is the 1975 Asia-Pacific Trade Agreement, APTA) and was **not** renamed "BISTEC" in 2014.
[159:3:4] - Sectors **cut from 14 to 7** (not expanded); each has a lead country. India leads **security** (counter-terrorism, disaster management, energy). Under the old 14 sectors **Myanmar** led **energy** (and agriculture).
[159:3:5] # Summits
[159:3:6] TABLE head: Summit | Venue / date | Outcome
  [159:3:6.0] 1st | Bangkok, 31 July 2004 | –
  [159:3:6.1] 4th | Kathmandu, 31 Aug 2018 | Grid Interconnection MoU signed
  [159:3:6.2] 5th | 30 March 2022, virtual, hosted by Sri Lanka (Colombo); addressed by PM Modi | BIMSTEC Charter adopted; Sri Lanka chair (India did not chair it)
  [159:3:6.3] 6th | Bangkok, 4 April 2025 (Thailand chair; PM Modi attended) | Bangkok Vision 2030 ("Prosperous, Resilient and Open"); Maritime Transport Cooperation Agreement; India's 21-point Action Plan
[159:3:7] - Current chair: **Bangladesh** (from April 2025).
[159:3:8] # Energy, security and India's initiatives
[159:3:9] - **Grid Interconnection MoU:** Union Cabinet approved signing on **12 April 2017** (**power sector** – cross-border electricity trade); signed at the 4th Summit (2018); set up the **BIMSTEC Grid Interconnection Coordination Committee (BGICC)**.
[159:3:10] - **First BIMSTEC Disaster Management Exercise (DMEx)** – **October 2017, hosted by India** (New Delhi/NCR).
[159:3:11] - **MILEX 2018, Pune** – first BIMSTEC army exercise.
[159:3:12] - **BIMSTEC leaders** were invited to PM Modi's **oath-taking, 30 May 2019**.
[159:3:13] - **First BIMSTEC Conference on Combating Drug Trafficking** – **13–14 February 2020, New Delhi**, hosted by **India** (NCB).
[159:3:14] - **1st BIMSTEC Youth Summit** – Gandhinagar (Feb 2025); **Cancer Care programme** at Tata Memorial (July 2025); **BODHI** training initiative (2025).
[159:3:15] TABLE head: BIMSTEC centre | Location
  [159:3:15.0] Cultural Industries Observatory | Thimphu
  [159:3:15.1] Energy Centre | Bengaluru (Host Country Agreement Nov 2024)
  [159:3:15.2] Centre for Weather and Climate | Noida
  [159:3:15.3] Technology Transfer Facility | Colombo
[159:3:16] - Weaknesses: few summits, weak Secretariat, slow FTA, Myanmar crisis.
[159:3:17] > Exam angle: Secretariat **Dhaka**; 5th Summit host **Sri Lanka**; 6th Summit **Bangkok 2025**; centres → cities (UPSC 2026 match).
[159:3:18] ~~ Sources: PMF IAS International Relations p.117-119 (src/international-relations/pmf-international-relations.txt); PIB/Business Standard 12 April 2017 (Cabinet nod to BIMSTEC Grid Interconnection MoU); MHA/NCB release 13 Feb 2020 (BIMSTEC drug-trafficking conference, New Delhi); app notes 2:159 sub 3
## SUB 4 | NATO, Commonwealth and Other Bodies
[159:4:0] # NATO
[159:4:1] TABLE head: Event | Date
  [159:4:1.0] Founded (Washington Treaty) | 1949
  [159:4:1.1] Croatia and Albania join | 2009
  [159:4:1.2] North Macedonia – 30th | Protocol signed Feb 2019; member 27 March 2020
  [159:4:1.3] Finland – 31st | 4 April 2023 (not February)
  [159:4:1.4] Sweden – 32nd | 7 March 2024
[159:4:2] - **Russia is not** a NATO member; in **May 2002** it joined the **NATO-Russia Council** (Rome) – the basis of the APPSC 2002 key "Russia". France, Germany and Britain are founder/old members.
[159:4:3] - **Türkiye** has **not** left NATO (UPSC 2024 wrong pair).
[159:4:4] # Commonwealth
[159:4:5] - **London Declaration 1949**; Secretariat London; **Secretary-General: Shirley Ayorkor Botchwey** (Ghana).
[159:4:6] - **Head:** **Queen Elizabeth II** until 8 September 2022 → now **King Charles III**.
[159:4:7] - Membership is **voluntary** – former British territories did not join automatically (e.g., Myanmar never joined).
[159:4:8] - It had **no charter** until the **Commonwealth Charter** (adopted December 2012, signed **11 March 2013**).
[159:4:9] # Other organisations
[159:4:10] TABLE head: Body | Facts
  [159:4:10.0] APEC | Set up 1989, Canberra; 21 Pacific Rim economies; India is not a member
  [159:4:10.1] OIC | 14th Islamic Summit – Mecca (Makkah), 31 May 2019
  [159:4:10.2] Nordic Council | 1952: Denmark, Finland, Iceland, Norway, Sweden – not the UK
  [159:4:10.3] CICA | Almaty Charter, June 2002 – 16-nation summit (India, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Palestine signed); Thailand was not a signatory
  [159:4:10.4] OECD | Paris; India is not a member
  [159:4:10.5] USMCA | USA, Mexico, Canada – replaced NAFTA in 2020; first joint review July 2026
[159:4:11] > Exam angle: NATO's newest members and dates; "India is a member of ___" (IORA, EAS – yes; APEC, OECD – no).
[159:4:12] ~~ Sources: PMF IAS International Relations p.131-132 (NATO) (src/international-relations/pmf-international-relations.txt); app notes 2:159 sub 0, 7; general knowledge for APEC, OIC, Nordic Council, CICA, Commonwealth Charter (not checked)
## SUB 5 | SCO, GCC, IORA and African Union
[159:5:0] # Shanghai Cooperation Organisation
[159:5:1] - **Shanghai Five (1996)** → **SCO 2001**; Secretariat **Beijing**; **RATS** (Regional Anti-Terrorist Structure) at **Tashkent**.
[159:5:2] - **10 members:** China, Russia, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Tajikistan, Uzbekistan, **India and Pakistan (2017, Astana)**, Iran (2023), **Belarus (2024 – newest)**.
[159:5:3] TABLE head: SCO summit | Venue
  [159:5:3.0] May 2003 | Moscow
  [159:5:3.1] 15–16 Sep 2022 | Samarkand, Uzbekistan
  [159:5:3.2] July 2024 | Astana – Belarus joined
  [159:5:3.3] 2025 (25th) | Tianjin, China
[159:5:4] # Gulf Cooperation Council
[159:5:5] - **1981**, HQ **Riyadh**; **6 members:** Bahrain, Kuwait, Oman, Qatar, Saudi Arabia, UAE – **Iran is not** a member.
[159:5:6] - **June 2017:** Saudi Arabia, UAE, Bahrain and Egypt (with others) cut ties with **Qatar**, citing its **support for terrorism and ties with Iran**; ended by the **Al-Ula Declaration (January 2021)**.
[159:5:7] # IORA
[159:5:8] - **Indian Ocean Rim Association** (1997, as IOR-ARC), Secretariat **Ebene, Mauritius**, **23** members; **India is a member**; **Nelson Mandela** was among the leaders behind its creation.
[159:5:9] - Not "very recent" and not set up against piracy or oil spills; **not a maritime-security-only** alliance.
[159:5:10] - **Priority areas:** maritime safety and security; trade and investment facilitation; fisheries management; disaster risk management; academic and scientific cooperation; tourism and cultural exchanges. Cross-cutting: blue economy, women's economic empowerment.
[159:5:11] - **India is chair (2025-27)**, taking over from Sri Lanka in Nov 2025; **summit 2027**, first since **Jakarta 2017** (20th anniversary).
[159:5:12] # African Union and AP angle
[159:5:13] - **African Union** (2002) replaced the **OAU** (1963); HQ **Addis Ababa**; G20 permanent member since 2023.
[159:5:14] - AP: **NACIN Palasamudram** (Rayalaseema) – a **World Customs Organization** regional training centre for officials of SAARC, ASEAN and African nations.
[159:5:15] > Exam angle: "not a GCC member" → **Iran**; RATS → SCO; IORA Secretariat Ebene.
[159:5:16] ~~ Sources: PMF IAS International Relations p.120-122 (SCO) (src/international-relations/pmf-international-relations.txt); app notes 2:159 sub 0, 7, 8
## SUB 6 | Quick Revision – Founding, HQ, Members
[159:6:0] TABLE head: Organisation | Founded | HQ / Secretariat | Members
  [159:6:0.0] SAARC | Dhaka, 8 Dec 1985 | Kathmandu | 8
  [159:6:0.1] BIMSTEC | 1997 Bangkok Declaration | Dhaka | 7
  [159:6:0.2] ASEAN | 1967 Bangkok Declaration | Jakarta | 11
  [159:6:0.3] EU | Maastricht 1992/93 | Brussels | 27
  [159:6:0.4] African Union | 2002 (OAU 1963) | Addis Ababa | 55
  [159:6:0.5] SCO | 2001 | Beijing; RATS Tashkent | 10
  [159:6:0.6] GCC | 1981 | Riyadh | 6
  [159:6:0.7] IORA | 1997 | Ebene, Mauritius | 23
  [159:6:0.8] Council of Europe | 1949 | Strasbourg | 46 (47 till 2022)
  [159:6:0.9] NATO | 1949 | Brussels | 32
  [159:6:0.10] MGC | 2000, Vientiane | – | 6
[159:6:1] - Newest members: ASEAN = **Timor-Leste**; EU = **Croatia**; NATO = **Finland + Sweden**; SCO = **Belarus**.
[159:6:2] - Two "Bangkok Declarations": **ASEAN 1967** and **BIMSTEC 1997**; BIMSTEC Charter **Colombo** (2022); BBIN MVA and SAARC Development Fund **Thimphu**.
[159:6:3] ~~ Sources: PMF IAS International Relations p.108-134; app notes 2:159 sub 0, 9, 11

=== OWN MCQs of 2:159 (78)
[nf8d1fc8a893] sub0
  Where is the headquarters of the Council of Europe, founded in 1949?
  Brussels | Geneva | *Strasbourg | Luxembourg
  x: The Council of Europe sits at Strasbourg; Brussels is the seat of the EU.
[n7cfe48dc464] sub0
  Which country was expelled from the Council of Europe in March 2022?
  *Russia | Türkiye | Azerbaijan | Belarus
  x: Russia was expelled from the Council of Europe, which had 47 members in 2019, in March 2022.
[g82ac7e192a02] sub0
  Under the Treaty of Rome, 1957, which forerunner of the European Union was set up?
  *European Economic Community | Council of Europe | European Free Trade Association | North Atlantic Treaty Organisation
  x: The Treaty of Rome (1957) set up the European Economic Community (EEC), the forerunner of the EU formed under the Maastricht Treaty.
[g7048557312e3] sub0
  How many member states did the Council of Europe have in 2019?
  27 | 41 | *47 | 49
  x: The Council of Europe (Strasbourg, 1949) had 47 members in 2019; Russia was expelled in March 2022.
[g5df4a67a55be] sub0
  Which treaty of 1992 created the European Union?
  Treaty of Rome | *Maastricht Treaty | Lisbon Treaty | Treaty of Nice
  x: The EU was founded by the Maastricht Treaty (1992, in force 1993); the Treaty of Rome (1957) set up the EEC.
[ncc7e37fd28a] sub0
  Brexit, the United Kingdom's withdrawal from the European Union, was carried out under which Article of the Treaty on European Union?
  Article 48 | *Article 50 | Article 49 | Article 51
  x: Article 50 TEU is the EU's exit clause; it was invoked for Brexit and the UK left on 31 January 2020.
[na9a9ab7a0d0] sub0
  Which country was the latest to join the European Union?
  Bulgaria | Romania | Slovenia | *Croatia
  x: Croatia was the last country to join the European Union, in 2013.
[g012ade3e2ad3] sub0
  In which year did the European Union adopt the Digital Single Market Strategy?
  2010 | 2013 | 2019 | *2015
  x: The EU adopted the Digital Single Market Strategy on 6 May 2015, with three pillars including better access to digital goods and services.
[gb1e4ee7dee49] sub0
  Which of the following is NOT correct about the Council of Europe?
  Its headquarters is at Strasbourg | It was founded in 1949 | *It is a body of the European Union | Russia was expelled from it in March 2022
  x: The Council of Europe is separate from the EU; it is based at Strasbourg and was founded in 1949.
[g388ecd72e576] sub0

  44 | 104 | *144 | 244
  x: In the India-EU FTA (2026), the EU made commitments in 144 services subsectors.

  23 June 2016 | 29 March 2019 | *31 January 2020 | 31 December 2020
  x: Using Article 50 TEU, the UK left the EU on 31 January 2020, bringing the EU back to 27.

=== NOTES 2:160 | Plurilateral Groupings – G20, G7, Brics, Quad, I2U2, Ibsa, Basic, Colombo Security Conclave
## SUB 0 | BRICS
[160:0:0] # Origin and expansion
[160:0:1] TABLE head: Year | Event
  [160:0:1.0] 2001 | Term "BRIC" coined by Jim O'Neill (Goldman Sachs)
  [160:0:1.1] 2006 | BRIC formalised (foreign ministers)
  [160:0:1.2] 2009 | First summit, 16 June, Yekaterinburg (Russia) – Brazil, Russia, India, China
  [160:0:1.3] 2010-11 | South Africa invited Dec 2010; first attended at Sanya, China (2011)
  [160:0:1.4] June 2022 | Iran and Argentina applied to join
  [160:0:1.5] Aug 2023 | 15th Summit, Johannesburg: six invited – Argentina, Egypt, Ethiopia, Iran, Saudi Arabia, UAE; Argentina declined (Dec 2023)
  [160:0:1.6] 1 Jan 2024 | Egypt, Ethiopia, Iran, UAE joined
  [160:0:1.7] 2025 | Indonesia joined
[160:0:2] - Members now **10**; **Saudi Arabia's** status ambiguous (listed by some sources, yet to formally join, 14 Sep 2026).
[160:0:3] - **BRIC in 2010:** China's GDP was more than the combined GDP of Brazil, Russia and India; but India + Brazil together outnumber China's population (UPSC 2010: A only).
[160:0:4] - India is a **founder**; India **avoids de-dollarisation**. Debate: "anti-West" (Russia, China, Iran) vs "non-West" (India, Brazil, South Africa).
[160:0:5] # Summits and chairs (B-R-I-C-S rotation)
[160:0:6] TABLE head: Summit | Year | Chair / venue
  [160:0:6.0] 5th | March 2013 | South Africa – Durban
  [160:0:6.1] 8th | 2016 | India (chair Feb–Dec 2016; Goa)
  [160:0:6.2] 9th | 2017 | China – Xiamen
  [160:0:6.3] 11th | 13–14 Nov 2019 | Brazil – Brasília
  [160:0:6.4] 12th | 2020 | Russia – virtual (St Petersburg meet of July 2020 postponed by COVID-19)
  [160:0:6.5] 14th | 2022 | China – virtual
  [160:0:6.6] 16th | Oct 2024 | Russia – Kazan
  [160:0:6.7] 17th | 2025 | Brazil – Rio de Janeiro
  [160:0:6.8] 18th | 12 Sep 2026 | India – New Delhi, Bharat Mandapam
  [160:0:6.9] 19th | 2027 | China
[160:0:7] - India's 2026 theme: **"Building for Resilience, Innovation, Cooperation and Sustainability"**; earlier Indian hostings 2012, 2016, 2021 (virtual).
[160:0:8] - **BRICS New Delhi Declaration (Sep 2026)**, by consensus despite the Iran-UAE conflict: UNSC expansion (China, Russia back a greater role for India and Brazil); **IMF/WB quota reform**; restoring the **WTO Appellate Body**; West Asia de-escalation; zero tolerance on terrorism.
[160:0:9] # Institutions
[160:0:10] - **NDB** – agreed at **Fortaleza, 2014**; HQ **Shanghai**.
[160:0:11] - **Contingent Reserve Arrangement** (2014) – **$100 billion** liquidity pool; BRICS, **not Quad**.
[160:0:12] > Exam angle: venues and chairs year by year; who applied / joined when.
[160:0:13] ~~ Sources: PMF IAS International Relations p.112-116 (src/international-relations/pmf-international-relations.txt); app notes 2:160 sub 2
## SUB 1 | G20
[160:1:0] # Origin
[160:1:1] TABLE head: Item | Fact
  [160:1:1.0] Planned at | G7 Cologne summit, June 1999
  [160:1:1.1] Set up | 26 Sep 1999 as a forum of finance ministers and central bank governors (after the Asian Financial Crisis)
  [160:1:1.2] First meeting | Berlin, Dec 1999
  [160:1:1.3] First leaders' summit | Washington D.C., 2008 (global financial crisis)
[160:1:2] - **Members:** **19 countries** – Argentina, Australia, Brazil, Canada, China, France, Germany, India, Indonesia, Italy, Japan, **Mexico**, Russia, Saudi Arabia, **South Africa**, South Korea, **Turkey**, UK, US – + **EU** + **African Union** (permanent member since **2023**). **Not members:** Malaysia, New Zealand, Iran, Vietnam, Singapore.
[160:1:3] - **No permanent secretariat**; annual rotating presidency. **Troika:** previous + current + next presidencies; 2022 = **Indonesia, Italy, India**.
[160:1:4] - About **85% of world GDP, 75% of trade, two-thirds of population**.
[160:1:5] # Summits
[160:1:6] TABLE head: Year | Venue
  [160:1:6.0] 2018 | Buenos Aires, Argentina
  [160:1:6.1] 2019 | Osaka, Japan
  [160:1:6.2] 2020 | Riyadh (15th, virtual) – Saudi Arabia took the chair from Japan (Dec 2019)
  [160:1:6.3] 2022 | Bali, Indonesia – theme "Recover Together, Recover Stronger"
  [160:1:6.4] 2023 | 18th, New Delhi (Bharat Mandapam, 9-10 Sep)
  [160:1:6.5] 2024 | Rio de Janeiro
  [160:1:6.6] 2025 | Johannesburg, South Africa – first G20 summit in Africa
[160:1:7] # India's presidency (2022-23)
[160:1:8] - India took over from **Indonesia on 1 December 2022** – India's **maiden presidency**; first time India convened the Leaders' Summit (Sep 2023); **43 Heads of Delegations** – the largest ever in the G20.
[160:1:9] - Theme **"Vasudhaiva Kutumbakam – One Earth, One Family, One Future"**; **"Data for Development"** an integral part of it.
[160:1:10] - **New Delhi Declaration (2023):** **African Union** admitted as permanent member; **Global Biofuels Alliance** launched.
[160:1:11] - **Brisbane-eThekwini Goal** (revised, G20 Development Ministers 2025): cut gender gap in labour participation by **25% by 2030** from 2012.
[160:1:12] > Exam angle: host city by year; India's theme vs Indonesia's theme; members (Argentina, Mexico, South Africa, Turkey).
[160:1:13] ~~ Sources: PMF IAS International Relations p.124-127 (src/international-relations/pmf-international-relations.txt); app notes 2:160 sub 1
## SUB 2 | G7 / G8
[160:2:0] - **Members (7):** US, UK, France, Germany, Italy, Canada, Japan. The **EU attends** meetings. No treaty or secretariat.
[160:2:1] - **Group of Six 1975** → Canada joined **1976** → **Russia** joined 1997-98 → **G8** (France, Germany, Italy, Japan, UK, US, Canada, Russia); suspended **2014 after Crimea** → back to G7.
[160:2:2] - **Not members** (distractors): **Russia**, **Netherlands**, **New Zealand**, India, China.
[160:2:3] TABLE head: G7 summit | Venue
  [160:2:3.0] 1995 (21st) | Halifax, Canada
  [160:2:3.1] 2016 (42nd) | Ise-Shima, Japan
  [160:2:3.2] 2022 | Germany – PGII launched (counter to China's BRI)
  [160:2:3.3] 2025 (51st) | Kananaskis, Canada
  [160:2:3.4] June 2026 | France – PM Modi met President Trump
[160:2:4] - **India:** regular **outreach invitee**, not a member (2025 = sixth consecutive invitation).
[160:2:5] > Exam angle: "not a G7 member" → Russia / Netherlands / New Zealand; Halifax = G7.
[160:2:6] ~~ Sources: PMF IAS International Relations p.128-130 (src/international-relations/pmf-international-relations.txt); app notes 2:160 sub 0, 4
## SUB 3 | I2U2, IBSA, BASIC, Quad and Other Groupings
[160:3:0] # Groupings and India's status
[160:3:1] TABLE head: Grouping | Members | India
  [160:3:1.0] G20 | 19 + EU + AU | Member
  [160:3:1.1] G7 | 7 | Invitee only
  [160:3:1.2] BRICS | 10 | Founder
  [160:3:1.3] Quad | India, US, Japan, Australia (not China) | Member
  [160:3:1.4] I2U2 | India, Israel, UAE, US (first summit July 2022) | Member
  [160:3:1.5] IBSA | India, Brazil, South Africa | Member
  [160:3:1.6] BASIC | Brazil, South Africa, India, China | Member
  [160:3:1.7] G4 | India, Brazil, Germany, Japan (UNSC bid) | Member
  [160:3:1.8] MIKTA | Mexico, Indonesia, South Korea, Türkiye, Australia | Not member
  [160:3:1.9] TPP | 12 Pacific Rim nations | Not member
[160:3:2] - **Indonesia** is in BRICS (2025) and MIKTA, but **not** in the Quad or IBSA.
[160:3:3] # IBSA and BASIC
[160:3:4] - IBSA founded **2003, Brasilia Declaration**; **IBSA Trust Fund** (2004, run by **UNOSSC**); **IBSAMAR** naval exercise.
[160:3:5] - **5th IBSA Summit – Pretoria, 18 October 2011**; Leaders' meetings: Rio de Janeiro (Nov 2024), **Johannesburg (Nov 2025)** – UNSC expansion "not an option but an imperative".
[160:3:6] - India's proposals (Nov 2025): **IBSA Fund for Climate-Resilient Agriculture**, **IBSA Digital Innovation Alliance** (UPI, CoWIN), NSA-level dialogue.
[160:3:7] - **BASIC** formed **2009**, before **COP15 Copenhagen**; stand: **CBDR-RC**; climate finance from developed countries.
[160:3:8] # Quad
[160:3:9] - Origin: **Tsunami Core Group** after the **2004 Indian Ocean tsunami**; first meeting **2007**; **revived 2017**.
[160:3:10] - First leaders' summit – **virtual, March 2021**; first in-person – **Washington, Sep 2021**; Dec 2024 statement marked 20 years.
[160:3:11] - Initiatives: **Quad Vaccine Partnership**, **Critical and Emerging Technologies group**, **IPMDA** (2022).
[160:3:12] # Trans-Pacific Partnership
[160:3:13] - **12 Pacific Rim nations**; deal concluded **October 2015**, signed **4 February 2016** (Auckland); about **40% of world GDP**; **India was not** a member; the US left in 2017 → **CPTPP** (2018).
[160:3:14] # Colombo Security Conclave
[160:3:15] - Began **2011** as India-Sri Lanka-**Maldives** maritime trilateral; renamed CSC **2020**; secretariat **Colombo**.
[160:3:16] - **Members:** India, Sri Lanka, Maldives, **Mauritius**, **Bangladesh**; **Seychelles – observer**.
[160:3:17] - **Five pillars:** maritime safety and security; counter-terrorism and radicalisation; **trafficking and organised crime**; cyber security and critical infrastructure; **HADR**.
[160:3:18] # India-led initiatives
[160:3:19] TABLE head: Initiative | Facts
  [160:3:19.0] CDRI | Launched by India at the UN Climate Action Summit, New York, September 2019; HQ New Delhi
  [160:3:19.1] International Solar Alliance | India and France, COP21 Paris 2015; HQ Gurugram
  [160:3:19.2] Voice of Global South Summit | Virtual, 12-13 Jan 2023; theme "Unity of voice, Unity of purpose"
  [160:3:19.3] Minerals Security Partnership | US-led, June 2022; India joined June 2023
[160:3:20] > Exam angle: I2U2 = India, Israel, UAE, USA (asked twice); IBSA summit venues; CDRI launched by India.
[160:3:21] ~~ Sources: PMF IAS International Relations p.10, p.62 (I2U2), p.118 (IBSA, BASIC) (src/international-relations/pmf-international-relations.txt); app notes 2:160 sub 3, 5-8; general knowledge for TPP dates and 5th IBSA date (not checked)
## SUB 4 | Share of the World Economy
[160:4:0] TABLE head: Grouping | Share
  [160:4:0.0] G20 | about 85% of GDP, 75%+ of trade, two-thirds of population
  [160:4:0.1] Expanded BRICS | 40%+ of GDP (PPP), about 50% of population, about 26% of trade
  [160:4:0.2] BRICS in 2011 | 20% of GDP but only 11% of IMF votes
  [160:4:0.3] G7 | about 43% of nominal GDP (2023); about 30% in PPP (2024)
[160:4:1] - Country shares (around 2019-20, nominal): **Japan ~6%** of world GDP, China ~16%, India ~3%, Canada ~2%.
[160:4:2] - BRICS = **46% of global trade** is a wrong statement; "first summit 2009 in Russia" is correct.
[160:4:3] - Firsts: G20 first leaders' summit Washington 2008; first BRIC summit Yekaterinburg 2009; AU became the G20's second regional member after the EU.
[160:4:4] ~~ Sources: PMF IAS International Relations p.112, p.125, p.128; app notes 2:160 sub 4, 9

=== NOTES 2:161 | Export Control and Security Regimes – Npt, Ctbt, Nsg, Mtcr, Wassenaar, Australia Group, Fatf, Iaea
## SUB 0 | NPT, CTBT and the Nuclear-weapon States
[161:0:0] # NPT
[161:0:1] - **Signed 1968**, **in force 1970**, **extended indefinitely in 1995**; Review Conferences every **5 years**.
[161:0:2] - Recognises **five Nuclear Weapon States** – those that **tested before 1 January 1967**: **USA, Russia, UK, France, China** (= UNSC P5).
[161:0:3] - Of China, France, India, Israel, Pakistan: **only China and France** are NPT-recognised NWS (UPSC 2015, asked twice).
[161:0:4] - **North Korea left the NPT in 2003**; it claimed its **first hydrogen-bomb test on 6 January 2016**.
[161:0:5] - India rejects the NPT as discriminatory – **"nuclear apartheid"**; wants **time-bound, universal, non-discriminatory disarmament** (Rajiv Gandhi Action Plan, 1988).
[161:0:6] # CTBT
[161:0:7] - Adopted **1996**; bans **all nuclear explosions**; **not in force** – needs ratification by all **44 Annex-2 States** (signing alone is not enough).
[161:0:8] - India has **not signed** (no time-bound disarmament commitment; subcritical tests still allowed).
[161:0:9] - **Iraq signed on 19 August 2008** and ratified on 26 September 2013 – so "Iraq and India have not signed" was already false in 2009.
[161:0:10] # India's nuclear doctrine
[161:0:11] - **No First Use**, **credible minimum deterrence**, massive retaliation; Pokhran-II **1998**.
[161:0:12] > Exam angle: NPT NWS = tested before 1 Jan 1967 (China, France among the options); CTBT entry into force = 44 Annex-2 ratifications.
[161:0:13] ~~ Sources: PMF IR notes (src/international-relations/pmf-international-relations.txt) pdf p.11-12; https://www.ctbto.org/our-work/country-profile?name=Iraq (search result: signed 2008-08-19, ratified 2013-09-26); app notes 2:161 sub 3
## SUB 1 | NSG and the Export-control Regimes
[161:1:0] TABLE head: Regime | Year | Members | Controls | India
  [161:1:0.0] NSG ("London Club") | 1975 | 48 | Nuclear materials and technology | Not a member – only the 2008 India-specific waiver
  [161:1:0.1] MTCR | 1987 | 35 | Missiles and drones – 500 kg over 300 km | Member, June 2016
  [161:1:0.2] Wassenaar Arrangement | 1996, Vienna | 42 | Conventional arms and dual-use goods | 42nd member, December 2017
  [161:1:0.3] Australia Group | 1985 | – | Chemical and biological weapon precursors | 43rd member, 19 January 2018
  [161:1:0.4] Hague Code of Conduct | 2002 | – | Transparency on ballistic-missile proliferation | Joined
[161:1:1] - **NSG:** formed in reply to India's **1974 Pokhran-I** test; **voluntary, consensus-based**, no permanent secretariat; India's entry is blocked by the **consensus rule**.
[161:1:2] - **Membership gives access to the latest nuclear technologies**; it does **not** make a country an NPT member (UPSC 2018: only statement I correct).
[161:1:3] - The 2008 waiver (India-US Civil Nuclear Deal) allows civil nuclear trade – import of uranium and reactors – without joining the NPT.
[161:1:4] - **China and Pakistan** are outside both **Wassenaar and MTCR**; **North Korea** is outside MTCR.
[161:1:5] - **North Korea** admitted exporting missiles to **Pakistan and Iran** and asked the US for **compensation** to stop their further spread.
[161:1:6] - India = "responsible nuclear state": entry into **3 of the 4** regimes (MTCR, Wassenaar, Australia Group – not NSG).
[161:1:7] - **NSG** in a general GS paper usually = **National Security Guard** (MHA counter-terror force, 1984, "Black Cats"); in non-proliferation = Nuclear Suppliers Group.
[161:1:8] > Exam angle: wrong-pair questions swap years/controls (NSG 1975 nuclear; MTCR 1987 missiles); India is in MTCR, Wassenaar, AG but not NSG.
[161:1:9] ~~ Sources: PMF IR notes (src/international-relations/pmf-international-relations.txt) pdf p.11, p.61; PMF Internal Security (src/disaster-mgmt/pmf-internal-security-dm-02.txt) p.23 (NSG force, 1984); app notes 2:161 sub 0-2
## SUB 2 | Arms-control Treaties, JCPOA and ICAN
[161:2:0] TABLE head: Treaty | Parties | Status
  [161:2:0.0] JCPOA (2015) | Iran with P5+1 (US, UK, France, Russia, China, Germany) and EU | US left 2018; Iran dropped all limits 5 January 2020
  [161:2:0.1] New START (signed April 2010, Prague) | USA and Russia (not China) | Bilateral strategic nuclear arms reduction; 1,550 deployed warheads; Russia suspended 2023; expired February 2026
  [161:2:0.2] INF Treaty (1987) | USA and USSR | US left 2019
  [161:2:0.3] CCW (1980) | UN convention on inhumane conventional weapons | India party to all five protocols
[161:2:1] - **START** = **Strategic Arms Reduction Treaty**; New START was the last US-Russia nuclear arms-control pact; **US + Russia** hold about **90%** of world arsenals.
[161:2:2] - **Iran:** US and Israel struck its nuclear sites **June 2025**; Iran's Parliament weighed **NPT exit** (Mar 2026).
[161:2:3] - **Nobel Peace Prize 2017: ICAN** (International Campaign to Abolish Nuclear Weapons) – for its work behind the **TPNW**.
[161:2:4] # TPNW, FMCT and nuclear-weapon-free zones
[161:2:5] - **TPNW:** first binding ban on nuclear weapons; **in force 2021**; **India did not sign** – says it adds nothing to customary international law.
[161:2:6] - **FMCT:** **India supports** negotiation at the **Conference on Disarmament** ("India does not support FMCT" is false).
[161:2:7] TABLE head: NWFZ treaty | Region
  [161:2:7.0] Tlatelolco | Latin America
  [161:2:7.1] Rarotonga | South Pacific
  [161:2:7.2] Bangkok | Southeast Asia
  [161:2:7.3] Pelindaba | Africa
  [161:2:7.4] Semipalatinsk | Central Asia
[161:2:8] > Exam angle: New START = USA-Russia bilateral (2010); JCPOA = 2015, Iran's full withdrawal 5 Jan 2020; ICAN = Nobel 2017.
[161:2:9] ~~ Sources: PMF IR notes (src/international-relations/pmf-international-relations.txt) pdf p.11 (New START), p.82 (JCPOA); app notes 2:161 sub 4, 8; general knowledge for ICAN Nobel 2017 (not checked)
## SUB 3 | FATF
[161:3:0] - Set up **1989 by the G7**; secretariat at **Paris (OECD)**; standards against **money laundering and terror financing**.
[161:3:1] - **Grey list** = jurisdictions under **increased monitoring**; **black list** = **high-risk jurisdictions subject to a call for action** – Iran, North Korea, **Myanmar** (unchanged at the Oct 2026 plenary).
[161:3:2] - **Pakistan:** **grey list** from **June 2018** for deficiencies in **terror financing and money laundering**; kept there (June 2020 too) for failing its **27-point action plan**; removed **October 2022**.
[161:3:3] - Oct 2026: grey list fell from 22 to **18** – DRC, Monaco, Bulgaria, Côte d'Ivoire removed.
[161:3:4] - **India:** member since **2010**; mutual evaluation adopted **June 2024** → **"regular follow-up"**; also in the **APG** (Asia/Pacific Group on Money Laundering).
[161:3:5] > Exam angle: Pakistan = grey (not black) list, 27-point plan, terror financing + money laundering.
[161:3:6] ~~ Sources: PMF IR notes (src/international-relations/pmf-international-relations.txt) pdf p.132, p.155; app notes 2:161 sub 7
## SUB 4 | IAEA, OPCW, CWC/BWC and Headquarters
[161:4:0] - **IAEA:** HQ **Vienna**, set up **1957**; India a **founding member**; DG **Rafael Grossi** (also chaired the NSG in 2016).
[161:4:1] TABLE head: Agreement | Year | Provision
  [161:4:1.0] NSG India-specific waiver | 2008 | Civil nuclear trade
  [161:4:1.1] India-Specific Safeguards Agreement | 2009 | Covers only civilian facilities, excludes military
  [161:4:1.2] Additional Protocol | in force 2014 | IAEA safeguards
  [161:4:1.3] India-Canada uranium | 2026 | 10,000 tonnes (2027-2035) for PHWRs
  [161:4:1.4] India-Australia uranium | July 2026 | Administrative Arrangement; Australia holds over one-third of world uranium
[161:4:2] # CWC and BWC
[161:4:3] TABLE head: Feature | CWC | BWC
  [161:4:3.0] Year | Signed 1993, in force 1997 | Adopted 1972, in force 1975
  [161:4:3.1] Verification body | OPCW | None
  [161:4:3.2] India | Party; destroyed its declared stockpile by 2009 | Party
[161:4:4] - **OPCW:** HQ **The Hague**; **Nobel Peace Prize 2013**; **not an EU body**; organs = Conference of States Parties, Executive Council, Technical Secretariat (no Board of Governors).
[161:4:5] TABLE head: Organisation | HQ
  [161:4:5.0] IAEA | Vienna
  [161:4:5.1] ITU | Geneva
  [161:4:5.2] Council of the EU | Brussels
  [161:4:5.3] OECD | Paris
  [161:4:5.4] OPCW | The Hague

=== OWN MCQs of 2:161 (61)
[n7e9b30c9298] sub0
  In which year was the NPT, which entered into force in 1970, extended indefinitely?
  1985 | 1990 | *1995 | 2005
  x: The NPT (signed 1968, in force 1970) was extended indefinitely in 1995.
[nd570d17ec0a] sub0
  India's principal objection to the NPT is that it:
  prohibits all civilian nuclear cooperation | does not contain any provision on nuclear energy | *is discriminatory and perpetuates nuclear apartheid | recognises India as a nuclear-weapon state without conditions
  x: India rejects the NPT as discriminatory, describing its structure as nuclear apartheid. India also seeks a time-bound disarmament plan from the nuclear powers before signing.
[n9724ad6af83] sub0
  India has not signed the Comprehensive Nuclear-Test-Ban Treaty (CTBT) primarily because it:
  *lacks a time-bound disarmament commitment and permits subcritical tests | recognises too many nuclear-weapon states | does not ban any form of nuclear explosion | applies only to chemical weapons
  x: India objects to the CTBT because it lacks a time-bound disarmament plan and still permits subcritical tests. The CTBT bans all nuclear explosions and was adopted in 1996.
[n49fcd8f2681] sub0
  In which year did North Korea withdraw from the Nuclear Non-Proliferation Treaty (NPT)?
  1998 | 2001 | *2003 | 2006
  x: North Korea left the NPT in 2003.
[fa59c33e7278b] sub0
  The NPT recognises as Nuclear Weapon States those that developed and tested a nuclear device before which date?
  *1 January 1967 | 1 January 1970 | 1 January 1968 | 1 January 1975
  x: NPT NWS are the states that tested before 1 January 1967: US, Russia, UK, France and China.
[fdc34f8e0898f] sub0
  How often are NPT Review Conferences held?
  2 years | *5 years | 4 years | 10 years
  x: NPT Review Conferences are held every five years, and the consensus rule lets single states block outcomes.
[gc36b0db82c81] sub0
  In which year was the Comprehensive Nuclear-Test-Ban Treaty (CTBT) adopted?
  1968 | 1987 | *1996 | 2002
  x: The CTBT was adopted in 1996 and bans all nuclear explosions, but it is not yet in force.
[nf26b4870f69] sub1
  Which of the following is wrongly matched?
  *NSG – 1987 – Missiles and drones | CTBT – 1996 – All nuclear tests | NPT – 1968/1970 – Spread of nuclear weapons | MTCR – 1987 – Missiles and drones (500 kg over 300 km)
  x: The NSG (1975) controls nuclear materials and technology; missiles and drones fall under the MTCR (1987).
[ne22ad7d3667] sub1
  The Hague Code of Conduct (2002) deals with transparency on which of the following?
  Conventional arms and dual-use goods | *Ballistic-missile proliferation | Chemical and biological weapon precursors | Nuclear materials and technology
  x: The Hague Code of Conduct concerns ballistic-missile transparency; Wassenaar covers conventional arms and the Australia Group chemical and biological precursors.
[nafe87e8d106] sub1
  Which pair of countries is outside both the Wassenaar Arrangement and the MTCR?
  India and Pakistan | *China and Pakistan | Russia and North Korea | Russia and Pakistan
  x: China and Pakistan are outside both Wassenaar and the MTCR; India is a member of both.
[f2e1607af71fa] sub1
  The MTCR seeks to control missiles and drones that can carry a payload of 500 kg over what range?
  150 km | *300 km | 500 km | 1,000 km
  x: The MTCR threshold is a 500 kg payload over 300 km.
[gfad05d6321ba] sub1
  Which export control regime deals with conventional arms and dual-use goods?
  Australia Group | *Wassenaar Arrangement | Nuclear Suppliers Group | Missile Technology Control Regime
  x: The Wassenaar Arrangement (1996, Vienna) controls conventional arms and dual-use goods, not nuclear items.
[gc9c86ec86fbe] sub1
  The Australia Group controls the export of which of the following?
  Nuclear materials and technology | Ballistic missiles | Conventional arms | *Chemical and biological weapon precursors
  x: The Australia Group (1985) controls chemical and biological weapon precursors.
[g030875dd87f7] sub1
  How many members does the Missile Technology Control Regime (MTCR) have?
  *35 | 42 | 48 | 29
  x: The MTCR (1987) has 35 members; the NSG has 48 and the Wassenaar Arrangement 42.
[g2c108950eb55] sub1
  Where is the Wassenaar Arrangement, set up in 1996, based?
  Geneva | *Vienna | The Hague | Paris
  x: The Wassenaar Arrangement (1996), which controls conventional arms and dual-use goods, is based at Vienna.
[g15f12a731f7d] sub1
  In which year was the Australia Group, which controls chemical and biological weapon precursors, formed?
  1975 | *1985 | 1987 | 1996
  x: The Australia Group was formed in 1985; India became its 43rd member in 2018.
[fd8723f28c44e] sub1
  When did India join the Missile Technology Control Regime (MTCR)?
  *June 2016 | December 2017 | January 2018 | June 2008
  x: India joined the MTCR in June 2016, the Wassenaar Arrangement in December 2017 and the Australia Group in January 2018.
[f59219edcb7c1] sub1
  India became the 43rd member of which regime on 19 January 2018?
  Wassenaar Arrangement | Nuclear Suppliers Group | *Australia Group | MTCR
  x: India became the Australia Group's 43rd member in January 2018; it was the 42nd Wassenaar member in December 2017.
[fe2b826c33305] sub1
  Which of the following export control regimes is India NOT a member of?
  MTCR | Wassenaar Arrangement | Australia Group | *Nuclear Suppliers Group
  x: India is in the MTCR, Wassenaar and the Australia Group, but not the NSG; it has only the 2008 India-specific waiver.
[g8bc917892d68] sub1
  In which year did India become the 42nd member of the Wassenaar Arrangement?
  2016 | 2018 | *2017 | 2008
  x: India joined the Wassenaar Arrangement as its 42nd member in December 2017.
[g988e2c80f2e2] sub1
  Consider the following statements:
1. India is a member of the Australia Group.
2. India is a member of the Nuclear Suppliers Group.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  *1 only | 2 only | Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: India joined the Australia Group in 2018 but is not in the NSG; it has only the 2008 India-specific waiver.
[n690798f859b] sub1
  In response to which event was the Nuclear Suppliers Group (NSG) formed in 1975?
  the creation of the OPCW | the signing of the CTBT | *India's 1974 Pokhran-I nuclear test | North Korea's 2003 NPT withdrawal
  x: The NSG was formed in 1975, popularly known as the London Club, in response to India's 1974 Pokhran-I test.
[n37a1ad3f372] sub1
  The Nuclear Suppliers Group, a voluntary and consensus-based body, has how many members?
  35 | 42 | 45 | *48
  x: The NSG has 48 members; 35 is the MTCR's count and 42 the Wassenaar Arrangement's.
[n7917488eeac] sub1
  What does the India-specific NSG waiver of 2008, associated with the India-US Civil Nuclear Deal, permit India to do?
  *trade in civilian nuclear material and import uranium and reactors | veto decisions of the NSG | join the NPT as a nuclear-weapon state | export nuclear weapons without safeguards
  x: The 2008 waiver permits civil nuclear trade and the import of uranium and reactors by India. It does not make India an NPT member.
[f6a4a90c21463] sub1
  By what name is the Nuclear Suppliers Group also known?
  Vienna Club | Paris Club | *London Club | Hague Club
  x: The NSG, formed in 1975 after India's Pokhran-I test, is known as the London Club.
[f88019d9fc956] sub1
  What mainly blocks India's entry into the Nuclear Suppliers Group?
  *Consensus rule | Permanent secretariat | Membership cap of 48 | Link with the IAEA Board
  x: The NSG works by consensus, so a single member can block India's entry; it has no permanent secretariat.
[nf2f2b417aae] sub2
  India is party to how many of the protocols of the UN Convention on Certain Conventional Weapons (CCW)?
  Two | Three | Four | *All five
  x: The CCW deals with inhumane conventional weapons, and India is party to all five of its protocols.
[n01fb9c3e7db] sub2
  In which year did the United States withdraw from the JCPOA (Iran nuclear deal)?
  2015 | 2019 | *2018 | 2020
  x: The US left the JCPOA in 2018; the deal dates from 2015 and Iran dropped all limits on 5 January 2020.
[n000921f05a0] sub2
  Consider the following statements:
1. New START (2010) was a treaty between the USA and China.
2. The INF Treaty (1987) was concluded between the USA and the USSR, and the US withdrew from it in 2019.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  1 only | *2 only | Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: Statement 1 is wrong: New START was between the USA and Russia (Russia suspended it in 2023). Statement 2 is correct: INF (1987) was a USA-USSR treaty and the US withdrew in 2019. So 2 only (B); A, C and D misjudge one of the two.
[g56c15dc04003] sub2
  With which country did the P5+1 and the EU conclude the JCPOA in 2015?
  Iraq | North Korea | *Iran | Syria
  x: The JCPOA (2015) is the Iran nuclear deal between Iran and the P5+1 with the EU; the US left it in 2018.
[gdd57e49a37bd] sub2
  What is the full form of START?
  *Strategic Arms Reduction Treaty | Strategic Arms Restriction Talks | Security and Arms Regulation Treaty | Strategic Atomic Reduction Talks
  x: START is the Strategic Arms Reduction Treaty; New START (2010) was between the USA and Russia.
[gf3ffa64007bc] sub2
  The USA and Russia together hold about what share of the world's nuclear arsenals?
  50% | 70% | *90% | 99%
  x: The US and Russia hold about 90% of global nuclear arsenals.
[n082e244d1df] sub2

  *Adds nothing to customary international law | Is backed by all nuclear-armed states | Should replace the NPT | Bans only conventional weapons

[nbe3c331bb46] sub2

  2017 | 2019 | *2021 | 2023
  x: The TPNW, a binding treaty banning nuclear weapons, entered into force in 2021.
[n2664d611632] sub2

  UN Security Council | IAEA Board of Governors | *Conference on Disarmament | Nuclear Suppliers Group
  x: India supports FMCT negotiations at the Conference on Disarmament, for a treaty that does not cap existing stocks.
[n349bc73708d] sub2
  In which region did the Treaty of Rarotonga set up a nuclear-weapon-free zone?
  Latin America | Southeast Asia | *South Pacific | Africa
  x: Rarotonga covers the South Pacific; Tlatelolco covers Latin America, Bangkok Southeast Asia and Pelindaba Africa.
[gd683d3629c4e] sub2
  The Treaty of Pelindaba established a nuclear-weapon-free zone in which region?
  Latin America | *Africa | Central Asia | South Pacific
  x: Pelindaba covers Africa; Tlatelolco is Latin America, Rarotonga the South Pacific and Semipalatinsk Central Asia.
[g57c8c1a17ecc] sub2
  The Treaty of Tlatelolco established a nuclear-weapon-free zone in which region?
  Africa | South Pacific | *Latin America | Central Asia
  x: Tlatelolco covers Latin America; Rarotonga the South Pacific, Pelindaba Africa, Semipalatinsk Central Asia.
[g5c71aef78b25] sub2
  Which treaty set up a nuclear-weapon-free zone in Central Asia?
  Treaty of Bangkok | *Treaty of Semipalatinsk | Treaty of Rarotonga | Treaty of Pelindaba
  x: The Semipalatinsk Treaty covers Central Asia; the Bangkok Treaty covers Southeast Asia.
[n9f5595b86d0] sub3
  Under the FATF, a 'call for action' applies to jurisdictions placed on which list?
  Grey list | *Black list | Both grey and black lists | Neither list
  x: The black list covers high-risk jurisdictions subject to a call for action; the grey list only means increased monitoring.
[n8254c927419] sub3
  Pakistan was kept on the FATF grey list from June 2018 to October 2022 mainly for failing to complete which of the following?
  *a 27-point action plan on terror financing | a 40-point recommendation on banking secrecy | the IMF Extended Fund Facility conditions | its mutual evaluation under the Asia/Pacific Group
  x: Pakistan stayed on the grey list (June 2018 to October 2022) for failing its 27-point action plan on terror financing. The other options are not the stated reason; the IMF EFF is unrelated to FATF listing and the APG is a regional body, not the cause of grey-listing.
[fcee2882ce7f6] sub3
  In which category did India's FATF mutual evaluation, adopted in June 2024, place India?
  Enhanced follow-up | Increased monitoring | *Regular follow-up | Call for action
  x: India's mutual evaluation adopted in June 2024 placed it in 'regular follow-up'.
[fd34f67b07bff] sub3
  Which of the following countries is on the FATF black list?
  Pakistan | Monaco | Bulgaria | *Myanmar
  x: The black list comprises Iran, North Korea and Myanmar; Monaco and Bulgaria were removed from the grey list and Pakistan left the grey list in 2022.
[fa65485a0fe99] sub3
  In which year did India become a member of the FATF?
  1989 | *2010 | 2018 | 2024
  x: India has been an FATF member since 2010; the FATF itself was set up by the G7 in 1989.
[g7d9812185c6f] sub3
  Which grouping set up the Financial Action Task Force (FATF) in 1989?
  G20 | *G7 | OECD | UN Security Council
  x: The FATF was set up in 1989 by the G7; its secretariat is at Paris (OECD).

  Besides the FATF, India is a member of which FATF-style regional body?
  Eurasian Group | *Asia/Pacific Group on Money Laundering | MENAFATF | Egmont Group
  x: India is in the Asia/Pacific Group on Money Laundering (APG), a FATF-style regional body.
[nbf6f1efcec4] sub3
  Where is the secretariat of the Financial Action Task Force (FATF) located?
  *Paris | Vienna | Geneva | Brussels
  x: The FATF secretariat is at Paris (OECD); Vienna hosts the IAEA.

[n5d10041a5fa] sub4

[n40dd4c7c234] sub4

  It implements the Chemical Weapons Convention | It inspects the destruction of chemical weapons | *It is an institution of the European Union | It received the Nobel Peace Prize in 2013
  x: The OPCW is not an EU body; it implements the CWC, monitors the chemical industry and won the 2013 Nobel Peace Prize.
[neb06cfe8716] sub4

  1972 | *1975 | 1993 | 1997
  x: The BWC was adopted in 1972, entered into force in 1975 and has no verification organisation.
[f32138b8a60cf] sub4

2. The BWC is verified by the OPCW.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  *1 only | 2 only | Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: The CWC was signed in 1993 and came into force in 1997; the BWC has no verification body, the OPCW serving only the CWC.
[ffde6abe6719b] sub4

  2009 | *2013 | 1997 | 2017

[geb0590cbaf2c] sub4

## SUB 1 | Border Management, CAPFs and Coastal Security
[164:1:0] - **Department of Border Management** (2004) – **Ministry of Home Affairs**; MHA controls all **CAPFs** ("One Border, One Force").
[164:1:1] - **Ministry of Defence** controls the Army (LoC, LAC) and the **Coast Guard**.
[164:1:2] TABLE head: Force | Border / role | Raised
  [164:1:2.0] BSF | Pakistan and Bangladesh | 1 Dec 1965
  [164:1:2.1] ITBP | China (LAC), 3,488 km, Karakoram Pass to Jachep La | 1962
  [164:1:2.2] SSB | Nepal and Bhutan; newest border force | 1963
  [164:1:2.3] Assam Rifles | Myanmar; oldest paramilitary; HQ Shillong | 1835
  [164:1:2.4] CRPF | Internal security, LWE; under MHA; began 27 July 1939 as Crown Representative's Police | 1939
  [164:1:2.5] CISF | Critical infrastructure, airports; under MHA; Infosys = first private company with CISF cover (July 2009) | 1969
  [164:1:2.6] Coast Guard | Territorial waters; under MoD (not Navy, not MHA) | 1977
[164:1:3] - **Paramilitary forces** (old list): **Assam Rifles, Indian Coast Guard**, SFF – not the Directorate General of Resettlement.
[164:1:4] - **Indian Coast Guard** = central coordinating agency for **marine oil-spill** response.
[164:1:5] - **Home Guards:** raised under **State/UT Home Guards Acts** (not a central Act); **auxiliary to the police** for internal security; **Border Wing Home Guards battalions** in border States (UPSC 2023: only two correct).
[164:1:6] - **Police and Public Order** = State List, **Seventh Schedule**; BSF jurisdiction in Punjab and West Bengal 15 km → **50 km**.
[164:1:7] - **Post-26/11 tiers:** Navy – overall; Coast Guard – territorial waters; coastal police – shallow waters. **Sagar Kavach** biannual exercise.
[164:1:8] - **CIBMS** smart fencing (Pakistan, Bangladesh); **Vibrant Villages** I – China border; **VVP-II (2024-25 to 2028-29)** – other land borders; **PM-DevINE** for the North East.
[164:1:9] - AP: 6 marine police stations sanctioned in Phase I (2005) of the Coastal Security Scheme.
[164:1:10] TABLE head: Neighbour | km
  [164:1:10.0] Bangladesh | 4,096.7 (longest)
  [164:1:10.1] China | 3,488
  [164:1:10.2] Pakistan | 3,323
  [164:1:10.3] Afghanistan | 106 (shortest)

## SUB 2 | Defence Set-up – CDS, Commands, Institutions, Agnipath
[164:2:0] - **CDS:** post created **Dec 2019**; first **Gen Bipin Rawat (1 Jan 2020)**; now **Gen Anil Chauhan**; heads the **Department of Military Affairs**; **Permanent Chairman, Chiefs of Staff Committee**; member of **Defence Planning Committee**; **principal military adviser to the Defence Minister** on tri-service matters; **maximum age 65**.
[164:2:1] - The **Defence Minister**, not the CDS, chairs the **Defence Acquisition Council**.
[164:2:2] - Before the CDS, the **senior-most Service Chief** chaired the Chiefs of Staff Committee.
[164:2:3] - **Agnipath (2022):** **four-year** recruitment of Agniveers.
[164:2:4] - Army commands: Northern, Western, **South Western**, Southern, Eastern, **Central**, **ARTRAC (Army Training Command)** – there is **no "South-Eastern Command"**. Navy: Western (Mumbai), Eastern (Vizag), **Southern (Kochi)**.
[164:2:5] TABLE head: Institution | Location
  [164:2:5.0] National Defence Academy | Khadakwasla, Pune
  [164:2:5.1] Army War College | Mhow
  [164:2:5.2] High Altitude Warfare School | Gulmarg
  [164:2:5.3] Army Air Defence College | Gopalpur, Odisha (not Pune)
  [164:2:5.4] RIMC | Dehradun
[164:2:6] - **Project Seabird:** greenfield naval base **INS Kadamba, Karwar** (west coast, Karnataka), commissioned 2005.

  [164:3:0.3] POTA | 2002, repealed 2004
  [164:3:0.4] NIA Act | 2008: Lok Sabha passed it 17 Dec 2008; with the UAPA amendment, assented 31 Dec 2008
  [164:3:0.5] Criminal Procedure (Identification) Act | 2022; rules notified Sept 2022 – police may collect physical and biological samples
  [164:3:0.6] BNS | Replaced IPC 1 July 2024; Section 113 = terrorist act

## SUB 5 | Left-wing Extremism, Greyhounds and the North-East
[164:5:0] - **People's War Group:** **Andhra Pradesh**; Kondapalli Seetharamaiah, 22 April 1980; merged into CPI (Maoist) 2004.
[164:5:1] - **Greyhounds:** AP's anti-Naxal force, raised **1989**; operational and training base **Anandapuram (Visakhapatnam)**; cut Naxal activity in Alluri Sitharama Raju and Parvathipuram Manyam; AOB.
[164:5:2] - **CoBRA** (Commando Battalion for Resolute Action) = **CRPF**'s anti-Naxal force; OCTOPUS = AP's counter-terror force; Salwa Judum = Chhattisgarh militia.
[164:5:3] - **Legacy Thrust Territories** (MHA): develop areas freed of LWE so it does not return.
[164:5:4] - **Manipur:** **Meitei vs Kuki**, since **May 2023**. NE accords – Bodo, Bru-Reang, NLFT.
[164:5:5] - Drug routes: **Golden Crescent** – Afghanistan, Iran, Pakistan; **Golden Triangle** – Myanmar, Laos, **Thailand**. Terror financing: hawala, **FICN** (fake Indian currency notes), narco-terror.
[164:5:6] > Exam angle: Greyhounds base = Visakhapatnam; CoBRA = CRPF.
[164:5:7] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:164 sub 2, 4, 7; general knowledge for CoBRA/OCTOPUS (not checked)
