  The ethnic conflict in Manipur has primarily been between which communities, beginning in May 2023?
  Kuki and Khasi | *Meitei and Kuki | Mizo and Meitei | Naga and Bodo
  x: The conflict in Manipur has primarily been between the Meitei and Kuki communities since May 2023.
[n785433d4b9e] sub5
  In which year was the Greyhounds force of Andhra Pradesh raised?
  1985 | *1989 | 1991 | 2004
  x: The Greyhounds, Andhra Pradesh’s special anti-Naxal force, were raised in 1989.
[nda68907f1c6] sub5
  “Legacy Thrust Territories” is a concept of the Ministry of Home Affairs intended to:
  Create new international border posts | Rehabilitate only surrendered insurgents | *Develop areas cleared of Left Wing Extremism so that it does not revive | Expand coastal security infrastructure
  x: The concept focuses on developing areas cleared of LWE to prevent the resurgence of extremism.
[f51196d77fd28] sub5
  Where is the base of Andhra Pradesh's Greyhounds force?
  *Anandapuram, Visakhapatnam | Mangalagiri, Guntur | Kurnool | Rajamahendravaram
  x: The Greyhounds, raised in 1989, are based at Anandapuram (Visakhapatnam).
[f6afd09839c2f] sub5
  Consider the following statements about the Greyhounds:
1. It is Andhra Pradesh's force against left-wing extremism.
2. It cut Naxal activity in Alluri Sitharama Raju and Parvathipuram Manyam districts.
3. It broke red corridors on the Andhra-Odisha Border.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  1 and 2 only | 2 and 3 only | 1 and 3 only | *1, 2 and 3
  x: All three are correct: the Greyhounds fight left-wing extremism, cut Naxal activity in those districts and broke red corridors on the AOB.
[nb1c3f0027f9] sub5
  Consider the following pairs:
1. Golden Crescent - Afghanistan, Iran and Pakistan
2. Golden Triangle - Myanmar, Laos and Bangladesh
Which of the pairs given above is/are correctly matched?
  *1 only | 2 only | Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: Pair 1 is correct: the Golden Crescent is Afghanistan, Iran and Pakistan. Pair 2 is wrong: the Golden Triangle is Myanmar, Laos and Thailand, not Bangladesh. So 1 only (A); B, C and D accept pair 2 or reject pair 1.
[f539143821391] sub5
  The Golden Triangle of drug production comprises Myanmar, Laos and which other country?
  Bangladesh | Cambodia | Vietnam | *Thailand
  x: The Golden Triangle is Myanmar, Laos and Thailand, not Bangladesh.
[fd7f097e0d97b] sub5
  In terror financing, what does FICN refer to?
  *Fake Indian currency notes | Foreign-funded insurgent cells | Funds moved through hawala | Narcotics smuggled across borders
  x: FICN is fake Indian currency, used alongside hawala and narcotics for terror financing.

=== NOTES 2:165 | Flagship Social Sector Programmes and Social Security – Health, Education, Nutrition, Housing, Pensions, Pds
## SUB 0 | Education Programmes – School, Literacy and Higher Education
[165:0:0] # School and teacher programmes
[165:0:1] TABLE head: Programme | Year | Objective
  [165:0:1.0] Operation Blackboard | 1987 (under NPE 1986) | Minimum infrastructure facilities in primary schools (rooms, blackboards, teaching aids, a second teacher), especially in educationally backward areas
  [165:0:1.1] RMSA (Rashtriya Madhyamik Shiksha Abhiyan) | 2009 | Universal access to quality secondary education; National Mission headed by the Union HRD Minister, Secretary (School Education and Literacy) as vice-chair
  [165:0:1.2] SWAYAM | 2017 | Free MOOCs (Massive Open Online Courses) from Class 9 to postgraduate
  [165:0:1.3] DIKSHA | Teachers' Day (5 Sep) 2017 | National Digital Infrastructure for Teachers; school content platform of NCERT
  [165:0:1.4] Samagra Shiksha | 2018 | Merged SSA, RMSA and Teacher Education; pre-school to Class 12
  [165:0:1.5] NIPUN Bharat | 2021 | Foundational literacy and numeracy by Grade 3
  [165:0:1.6] Vidyanjali | 7 Sep 2021 | School volunteer portal – links government and aided schools with volunteers, NGOs, CSR donors
  [165:0:1.7] PM SHRI | Announced Teachers' Day 2022; Cabinet 7 Sep 2022 | Upgrade 14,500+ schools into model schools showcasing NEP 2020 – modern infrastructure, smart classrooms, sports; Centrally Sponsored, 60:40 (2022-23 to 2026-27)
  [165:0:1.8] PM-Vidyalaxmi | 2024 | Collateral-free, guarantor-free loans for students admitted to top-quality HEIs
[165:0:2] - **SWAYAM = MOOCs for students; DIKSHA = teachers' platform** – both 2017.
[165:0:3] - **PM SHRI trap:** it was **not** announced on 26 January 2022 (announced September 2022) → statements a, b, c correct.
[165:0:4] - **Open Educational Resources (OER):** freely accessible, **openly licensed** documents and media for teaching, learning, assessment and research. **MOOC** = a course; **eGyanKosh** = IGNOU's repository; **Gyandarshan / Gyanvani** = educational TV / FM channels.
[165:0:5] # Adult literacy
[165:0:6] - **Adult education** aims at **literacy together with the learner's personal (functional) development**.
[165:0:7] - Order of programmes: **National Extension Service (1953)** → **National Adult Education Programme (2 Oct 1978)** → Adult Education Programme (recast, 1980s) → **Total Literacy Campaign** (under the **National Literacy Mission, 5 May 1988**; first in Kottayam/Ernakulam, Kerala).
[165:0:8] - **Saakshar Bharat** (**8 Sep 2009**): adult literacy, esp. women, in districts with female literacy below **50%**; Centre-State **75:25** (NE **90:10**).
[165:0:9] # Higher education
[165:0:10] - **RECs renamed NITs (2002)** under HRD Minister **Murli Manohar Joshi**; Institutes of National Importance from **2007**; NIT Warangal (1959) an early REC; **NIT Tadepalligudem** for AP.
[165:0:11] - **NIRF** parameters and weights: **Teaching, Learning and Resources 0.30**, **Research and Professional Practice 0.30**, Graduation Outcomes 0.20, Outreach and Inclusivity 0.10, Perception 0.10 → Research and Professional Practice is among the **heaviest**.
[165:0:12] - **Unnat Bharat Abhiyan** (2014; **2.0 in 2018**) – **MHRD** (now Ministry of Education): links IITs, NITs and other HEIs with villages for appropriate technology.
[165:0:13] > APPSC asks Operation Blackboard twice, MOOC twice, DIKSHA, PM SHRI statements and NIRF weight – learn the objective and the year.
[165:0:14] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:165 sub 3; Spectrum (src/indian-history/spectrum-modern-india.txt) p.760 (Operation Blackboard, NPE 1986); NextGen S&T Group 2 (src/science-tech/nextgen-st-group2-04.txt) p.5 (Unnat Bharat, MHRD, 2014); PMF LENS Sep 2026 (src/current-affairs/pmf-lens-2026-09-02.txt) p.59 (PM SHRI 14,500, 60:40); en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_educational_resources; epgp.inflibnet.ac.in (adult education chronology, NLM 1988)
## SUB 1 | Housing – PMAY-Gramin and PMAY-Urban
[165:1:0] # PMAY-Gramin
[165:1:1] - **Indira Awaas Yojana** (began **1985**, independent **1996**) was **restructured into PMAY-G with effect from 1 April 2016** – to **address the gaps** in IAY (poor targeting, small units, weak monitoring), not because of its name.
[165:1:2] - First aim: assist construction of **1 crore houses in 3 years** (2016-17 to 2018-19); overall target 2.95 crore by 2023-24 + **2 crore more** (2024-25 to **2028-29**).
[165:1:3] TABLE head: Feature | PMAY-G
  [165:1:3.0] Ministry | Rural Development
  [165:1:3.1] Minimum house size | 25 sq m (with a hygienic cooking space)
  [165:1:3.2] Unit assistance | ₹1.20 lakh (plains) / ₹1.30 lakh (hilly, NE)
  [165:1:3.3] Centre : State share | 60 : 40 (plains); 90 : 10 NE and Himalayan States; 100% UTs without legislature
  [165:1:3.4] Who builds | The beneficiary (with technical help; no contractor)
  [165:1:3.5] Monitoring | AwaasApp – geo-tagged, time-stamped photos for real-time, evidence-based progress
  [165:1:3.6] Convergence | MGNREGS (90/95 person-days of unskilled work), SBM-G toilet
[165:1:4] # PMAY-Urban
[165:1:5] - Launched **25 June 2015**: pucca house for **EWS/LIG and MIG** categories, including slum dwellers; original **Housing for All by 2022** target.
[165:1:6] - **Four components**: in-situ slum redevelopment; credit-linked subsidy; affordable housing in partnership; beneficiary-led construction (rural tap water is NOT one).
[165:1:7] - **PMAY-U 2.0 (2024)**: **1 crore** urban houses. Budget 2026-27 cut from ₹19,794 crore to ₹18,625 crore.
[165:1:8] - **Rajiv Awas Yojana**: aimed at a **slum-free India**; **subsumed into PMAY-U in 2015**.
[165:1:9] - Houses registered in women's names make PMAY a **gender-budget** scheme (26 Jan 2026).
[165:1:10] > Not offered in the options does not change the fact: PMAY-G share is **60:40** (not 75:25 or 90:10 for general States).
[165:1:11] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:165 sub 6; PMF LENS Apr 2026 (src/current-affairs/pmf-lens-2026-04-09.txt) p.11 (minimum unit size 25 sq m); static.pib.gov.in/WriteReadData/specificdocs/documents/2025/sep/doc2025924644801.pdf (60:40, 90:10, ₹1.20/1.30 lakh)
## SUB 2 | Health – Immunisation, Ayushman Bharat and Digital Health
[165:2:0] # Immunisation and health programmes
[165:2:1] TABLE head: Programme | Objective | Launch
  [165:2:1.0] Universal Immunisation Programme (UIP) | One of the largest public-health programmes – free vaccines for children and pregnant women | 1985
  [165:2:1.1] Pulse Polio | Polio eradication; WHO certified India polio-free in 2014 | 1995
  [165:2:1.2] Janani Suraksha Yojana (JSY) | Cash aid for institutional delivery; under NHM; 100% centrally sponsored | 2005
  [165:2:1.3] Mission Indradhanush | Full immunisation of children and pregnant women; a special catch-up campaign under the UIP; MoHFW | Dec 2014
  [165:2:1.4] Ayushman Arogya Mandir | Comprehensive primary care at about 1.5 lakh upgraded Health and Wellness Centres | 2018
  [165:2:1.5] PM-ABHIM | District critical-care blocks, Integrated Public Health Labs, BSL-3 labs; outlay ₹64,180 crore over 6 years | 2021
  [165:2:1.6] Sickle Cell Anaemia Elimination Mission | Eliminate sickle cell anaemia by 2047; launched at Shahdol (Madhya Pradesh) | July 2023
[165:2:2] - **NHM** has two sub-missions: **NRHM + NUHM**; **ASHAs and ANMs** are its backbone.
[165:2:3] - **Right to Health:** **Rajasthan** Assembly passed the **Right to Health Bill on 21 March 2023** – the first State to do so.
[165:2:4] # Ayushman Bharat PM-JAY
[165:2:5] TABLE head: Particular | Fact
  [165:2:5.0] Launch | 23 September 2018, Ranchi (Jharkhand)
  [165:2:5.1] Earlier name | National Health Protection Scheme
  [165:2:5.2] Subsumed | RSBY (2008)
  [165:2:5.3] Funding | 60:40; NE and Himalayan States 90:10
  [165:2:5.4] Implementing agency | National Health Authority (NHA); State Health Agencies
[165:2:6] - **₹5 lakh per family per year** (not ₹10 lakh) for **secondary and tertiary hospitalisation**; cashless and portable; **no cap on family size or age**; **3 days pre- and 15 days post-hospitalisation** expenses; a defined **transport allowance**.
[165:2:7] - **The world's largest** (not second-largest) health assurance scheme **fully financed by the government**; aims to cut **catastrophic expenditure**.
[165:2:8] - Eligibility: **SECC 2011** – **6 rural deprivation** and **11 urban occupational** criteria; **bottom 40%** (about **10.74 crore families**) – **poor and vulnerable in rural and urban areas** (APPSC key: "rural and poor populations").
[165:2:9] - **Oct 2024 – Ayushman Vay Vandana:** every person aged **70+**, **irrespective of income**, gets ₹5 lakh a year; extra ₹5 lakh top-up if the family is already covered.
[165:2:10] - AP's state scheme (begun as **Rajiv Aarogyasri, 2007**) now runs as **Dr NTR Vaidya Seva**, **converged with PM-JAY**; AP Universal Health Policy (2026): ₹2.5 lakh cover to all.
[165:2:11] # Digital health platforms
[165:2:12] TABLE head: Platform | Purpose
  [165:2:12.0] ABDM | National digital health ecosystem; launched September 2021 (pilot NDHM 15 Aug 2020)
  [165:2:12.1] ABHA | Unique health ID that links a person's health records
  [165:2:12.2] Healthcare Professionals Registry | Verified practitioners of modern and traditional medicine
  [165:2:12.3] Ni-kshay | Web portal to track TB patients
  [165:2:12.4] U-WIN | Registry of routine immunisation of pregnant women and children (modelled on CoWIN)
  [165:2:12.5] eSanjeevani | National telemedicine service (2019; first rolled out in Andhra Pradesh)
[165:2:13] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:165 subs 0-2; PMF General Science (src/science-tech/pmf-general-science-05.txt) p.7 (Pulse Polio 1995, polio-free 2014); Praveen CA360 Apr 2026 (src/current-affairs/praveen-ca360-2026-04-magazine.txt) p.82 (Mission Indradhanush 2014, JSY 2005); AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 (src/economy/ap-socio-economic-survey-2024-25.txt) p.225 (Aarogyasri trust)
## SUB 3 | Pensions, Insurance and Social Assistance
[165:3:0] # Government and armed-forces pensions
[165:3:1] - **OROP = One Rank One Pension**: same pension for armed-forces personnel retiring in the **same rank with the same length of service**.
[165:3:2] - **NPS**: from **1 January 2004** for new Central Government employees (**armed forces excluded**); **State employees** join from the date their State notifies it; open to **all citizens 18-70 (incl. NRIs, OCIs) from 1 May 2009** – so "resident citizens only" or "21-55 only" are wrong.
[165:3:3] - Regulator **PFRDA** (Act 2013); defined-contribution; PRAN; **Tier I mandatory, Tier II voluntary**; no account for a third person.
[165:3:4] - **UPS (2025)**: after **25 years**, **50%** of last 12 months' average basic pay; minimum ₹10,000. **NPS Vatsalya (2025)**: for **minors**.
[165:3:5] # Unorganised-sector pensions
[165:3:6] TABLE head: Scheme | Who | Age | Pension at 60 | Contribution
  [165:3:6.0] APY (9 May 2015; replaced Swavalamban) | Unorganised workers with bank account | 18-40 | ₹1,000-5,000 | Own; PFRDA
  [165:3:6.1] PM-SYM (2019; MoLE) | Workers earning ≤₹15,000/month | 18-40 | ₹3,000 | Beneficiary + Centre 1:1 (no State share)
  [165:3:6.2] PM-KMY (2019) | Small and marginal farmers | 18-40 | ₹3,000 | Equal matching by Centre
  [165:3:6.3] NPS-Traders (2019) | Traders, turnover ≤₹1.5 crore | 18-40 | ₹3,000 | Matching; NPS, ESIC, EPFO members and income-tax payers are NOT eligible
[165:3:7] - PM-SYM, PM-KMY, NPS-Traders: **spouse gets 50%** as family pension.
[165:3:8] - **Mukhyamantri Vishwakarma Pension Yojana** (workers, street vendors, folk artists) = **Rajasthan**.
[165:3:9] # Insurance schemes
[165:3:10] TABLE head: Scheme | Cover | Premium
  [165:3:10.0] PMJJBY (9 May 2015) | Life cover ₹2 lakh (death from any cause) | ₹436 a year
  [165:3:10.1] PMSBY (9 May 2015) | Accident: ₹2 lakh death/full disability; ₹1 lakh partial | ₹20 a year
  [165:3:10.2] Aam Aadmi Bima Yojana (2007) | Life and disability cover for rural landless households | –
[165:3:11] - **PMSBY data (26 April 2023):** cumulative enrolment **34.18 crore**; **₹2,302.26 crore** paid for **1,15,951 claims** (PMJJBY had about 16 crore).
[165:3:12] - **PM Vishwakarma** – launched **17 Sep 2023** (others: PMSBY 2015, MUDRA 2015, Ujjwala 2.0 2021).
[165:3:13] # Scheme–objective matching
[165:3:14] TABLE head: Scheme | Objective | Year
  [165:3:14.0] PMAY (Urban) | Affordable housing in cities | 2015
  [165:3:14.1] PMKVY | Skill development for youth | 2015
  [165:3:14.2] Ujjwala | Free LPG connections to BPL women | 1 May 2016, Ballia (UP)
  [165:3:14.3] Ayushman Bharat | Health insurance for poor families | 2018
[165:3:15] - **PMFBY** – crop insurance against natural disasters, **'One Nation-One Scheme'**.
[165:3:16] # NSAP and State pensions
[165:3:17] - **NSAP (1995)**: Ministry of **Rural Development**; gives effect to **Article 41** (DPSP); BPL households.
[165:3:18] TABLE head: Component | Eligibility | Central amount
  [165:3:18.0] IGNWPS | Widows 40-79 / 80+ | ₹300 / ₹500
  [165:3:18.1] IGNDPS | 18-79, disability ≥80% | ₹300 / ₹500
  [165:3:18.2] NFBS | Death of breadwinner aged 18-59 | ₹20,000 lump sum
  [165:3:18.3] Annapurna | Elderly eligible for IGNOAPS but not drawing it | 10 kg free grain
[165:3:19] - AP's **NTR Bharosa**: weavers, fishermen, toddy tappers, artisans, dappu artists; **₹4,000 a month**.
[165:3:20] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:165 subs 7-8; Laxmikanth (src/polity/laxmikanth-indian-polity-03.txt) p.246 (UPSC PYQ set); PMF LENS May 2026 (src/current-affairs/pmf-lens-2026-05-16.txt) p.7 (NFBS ₹20,000, IGNDPS 80%); Rocket CA 2026-02 (src/current-affairs/rocket-ca-2026-02.txt) p.26 (NSAP components); Praveen CA360 May 2026 p.32 (PMSBY, PMJJBY, APY 9 May 2015) and Sep 2026 p.22 (PM Vishwakarma 17 Sep 2023); static.pib.gov.in/WriteReadData/specificdocs/documents/2023/may/doc202359196601.pdf (PMSBY 34.18 crore, 26 Apr 2023)
[165:3:21] - **Chronology:** Aam Aadmi Bima Yojana (2007) → APY (2015) → PM-JAY (2018) → e-Shram (2021).
## SUB 4 | Labour Social Security – ESI, EPF, Code and e-Shram
[165:4:0] # ESI and EPF
[165:4:1] TABLE head: Feature | EPF | ESI
  [165:4:1.0] Law | EPF Act 1952 | ESI Act, 1948 (ESIC set up 1952)
  [165:4:1.1] Coverage | Establishments with 20+ employees | Employees earning up to the ceiling
  [165:4:1.2] Wage ceiling | ₹25,000 (raised from ₹15,000; 2026) | ₹21,000 a month (from ₹15,000, w.e.f. 1 Jan 2017)
  [165:4:1.3] Contribution | 12% each | Employer 3.25%, employee 0.75%
  [165:4:1.4] Benefits | PF + EPS pension + EDLI insurance | Medical, sickness, maternity, disablement, dependants'
[165:4:2] - ₹21,000 = ESI ceiling; ₹25,000 / ₹15,000 = EPF ceilings (new / old).
[165:4:3] - **EPF Scheme, 2026** replaced EPF Scheme 1952; **Aadhaar + PAN + UAN** linking mandatory.
[165:4:4] # Code on Social Security, 2020
[165:4:5] - Merged **9 laws** (EPF, ESI, Maternity Benefit, Gratuity, Unorganised Workers' Social Security Act, etc.); **all four Labour Codes in force 21 November 2025**.
[165:4:6] - **First legal recognition** of **gig and platform workers**; aggregators pay **1-2% of turnover**, capped at **5% of payouts**; **National Social Security Board**; maternity leave **26 weeks**; **creche at 50+** employees.
[165:4:7] # e-Shram
[165:4:8] - Launched **26 August 2021** by **MoLE**; Aadhaar-verified National Database of Unorganised Workers; **12-digit UAN**; age **16-59**; **excludes income-tax payers and EPFO/ESIC members**.
[165:4:9] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:165 sub 9; PMF LENS Mar 2026 (src/current-affairs/pmf-lens-2026-03-01.txt) p.58 (ESI cover up to ₹21,000); Praveen CA360 Jul 2026 (src/current-affairs/praveen-ca360-2026-07-magazine.txt) p.38 (ESIC 1952, MoLE)
## SUB 5 | Food Security and PDS
[165:5:0] # PDS evolution
[165:5:1] TABLE head: Phase | Year | Key change
  [165:5:1.0] Wartime rationing | 1940s (Bengal Famine 1943) | Import-dependent
  [165:5:1.1] FCI + Agricultural Prices Commission | 1965 | Supply side
  [165:5:1.2] Revamped PDS (RPDS) | June 1992 | First targeting of hilly, tribal and remote areas
  [165:5:1.3] Targeted PDS (TPDS) | June 1997 | BPL targeting
  [165:5:1.4] AAY and Annapurna | 2000 | Two special schemes for target groups – poorest households and destitute elderly
  [165:5:1.5] NFSA | 2013 | Legal right to food
  [165:5:1.6] ONORC | 2019 | Ration portability
[165:5:2] - **Antyodaya Anna Yojana** (Dec 2000): poorest of the poor; **35 kg per household** a month.
[165:5:3] - **Annapurna** (2000-01): **10 kg free grain** a month to elderly **eligible for IGNOAPS but not drawing it**.
[165:5:4] TABLE head: NFSA entitlement | Quantity
  [165:5:4.0] Priority households | 5 kg per person a month
  [165:5:4.1] AAY | 35 kg per household
  [165:5:4.2] Maternity benefit | ₹6,000
  [165:5:4.3] Coverage | 79.8 crore
[165:5:5] - **PMGKAY**: began 2020 (COVID-19); free NFSA grains for **5 years from 1 January 2024**.
[165:5:6] - **Food security (FAO):** physical and economic access for all to sufficient, safe, nutritious food; **not** 'food of one's choice'.
[165:5:7] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:165 sub 4; PMF LENS Aug 2026 (src/current-affairs/pmf-lens-2026-08-02.txt) p.68 (AAY 35 kg, PHH 5 kg); Tiss (src/polity/tiss.txt) scheme list (NSAP including Annapurna)
## SUB 6 | Nutrition, Sanitation and Drinking Water
[165:6:0] # Nutrition
[165:6:1] - **POSHAN Abhiyaan**: **8 March 2018, Jhunjhunu (Rajasthan)**; **Ministry of Women and Child Development**; converges **ICDS, NHM and Swachh Bharat**.
[165:6:2] - **Mission Poshan 2.0**: diet diversity, fortification, millets. **Poshan Maah = September**, **Poshan Pakhwada = March** (bi-annual, MWCD).
[165:6:3] # Sanitation
[165:6:4] - **Swachh Bharat Mission** launched **2 October 2014**; **SBM-G Phase II** → **ODF Plus** villages; **SBM-U 2.0** → garbage-free cities.
[165:6:5] - **Economic Survey 2016-17:** lack of toilets makes women **reduce water intake**.
[165:6:6] - **India Sanitation Coalition**: **25 June 2015**, set up by **FICCI**.
[165:6:7] - **NAMASTE**: ends hazardous **manual cleaning of sewers and septic tanks**; **MoSJE + MoHUA**; worker profiling, PPE, PM-JAY cover.
[165:6:8] # Drinking water
[165:6:9] - **Jal Jeevan Mission (2019)**: **Functional Household Tap Connection** for every rural home – original target **2024**, **extended to December 2028**; **Ministry of Jal Shakti**; **Village Water and Sanitation Committees** plan and run supply. ("Per Drop More Crop / Har Khet Ko Pani" = PMKSY.)
[165:6:10] TABLE head: Programme | Ministry
  [165:6:10.0] POSHAN Abhiyaan | Women and Child Development
  [165:6:10.1] NAMASTE | Social Justice + Housing and Urban Affairs
  [165:6:10.2] Jal Jeevan Mission | Jal Shakti
  [165:6:10.3] e-Shram, PM-SYM | Labour and Employment
  [165:6:10.4] NSAP | Rural Development
[165:6:11] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:165 sub 5; Praveen CA360 Mar 2026 (src/current-affairs/praveen-ca360-2026-03-magazine.txt) p.34 and PMF LENS Mar 2026 (src/current-affairs/pmf-lens-2026-03-02.txt) p.61 (JJM extended to Dec 2028)
## SUB 7 | Welfare Campaigns and Social Security Concepts
[165:7:0] TABLE head: Campaign | Target / focus | Lead ministry
  [165:7:0.0] Beti Bachao Beti Padhao | Sex ratio; girl child | Joint: MoWCD, MoHFW, MoE
  [165:7:0.1] Accessible India (Sugamya Bharat) | Persons with disabilities; audits public spaces | Social Justice
  [165:7:0.2] Vibrant Villages Programme | Northern border villages; reverse migration | Home Affairs
  [165:7:0.3] Nasha Mukt Bharat Abhiyaan | Youth substance abuse | Social Justice
[165:7:1] - **SEED** (2022): Central Sector scheme of **MoSJE** for **Denotified, Nomadic and Semi-Nomadic Tribes**; PM-JAY cover and PMAY housing.
[165:7:2] - Welfare programmes of social policy cover **employment, education, health, housing, food** – **not entertainment**.
[165:7:3] TABLE head: Social insurance | Social assistance
  [165:7:3.0] Contributory, tied to employment (EPF, ESI, APY, PM-SYM) | Non-contributory, tax-funded, means-tested (NSAP, NFSA, PM-JAY)
[165:7:4] - **ILO Convention No. 102** (1952): **nine branches** of social security; **India has not ratified it**.

=== OWN MCQs of 2:165 (155)

=== NOTES 2:166 | Flagship Programmes – Agriculture, Rural, Urban, Employment and Skilling
## SUB 0 | Saansad Adarsh Gram Yojana, MPLADS and DISHA
[166:0:0] # SAGY (Saansad Adarsh Gram Yojana)
[166:0:1] TABLE head: Feature | Fact
  [166:0:1.0] Launch | 11 October 2014 – birth anniversary of Lok Nayak Jayaprakash Narayan (not Gandhi Jayanti)
  [166:0:1.1] Ministry | Rural Development (not Panchayati Raj)
  [166:0:1.2] Other name | SAANJHI
  [166:0:1.3] Idea | MPs select Gram Panchayats and develop them into model villages (Adarsh Grams) using existing schemes – no separate fund
  [166:0:1.4] GP size | 3,000-5,000 population in plains; 1,000-3,000 in hilly, tribal, difficult areas
  [166:0:1.5] Target | One Adarsh Gram by 2016, two more by 2019, then five more by 2024
[166:0:2] - **Villages adopted (AP links):** **Sachin Tendulkar** (Rajya Sabha) – **Puttamraju Kandriga (P.R. Kandriga)**, Nellore district (2014); later **Donja**, Osmanabad (Maharashtra) (2016). **Suresh Prabhu** (Railways Minister, RS from AP) – **Pandrangi**, Visakhapatnam district (birthplace of Alluri Sitarama Raju).
[166:0:3] TABLE head: Scheme | Ministry | Key facts
  [166:0:3.0] MPLADS | MoSPI | Launched December 1993; funds non-lapsable (unspent funds carry forward); only durable assets; earmark 15% SC and 7.5% ST areas; district authority inspects at least 10% of works a year
  [166:0:3.1] DISHA (District Development Coordination and Monitoring Committee, 2016) | Rural Development | Chaired by the senior-most Lok Sabha MP of the district; Collector is member-secretary
[166:0:4] > UPSC 2020: the only wrong MPLADS statement is "sanctioned yearly, unused funds lapse".
[166:0:5] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:166 sub 3; OnlyIAS NCERT Economy (src/economy/onlyias-ncert-economy-05.txt) p.24 (SAGY, October 2014, MPs develop villages); Laxmikanth (src/polity/laxmikanth-indian-polity-03.txt) p.246 (UPSC 2020 MPLADS question)
## SUB 1 | DAY-NRLM, Rural Livelihoods and Rural Skilling
[166:1:0] # DAY-NRLM (MoRD; Centrally Sponsored)
[166:1:1] - **NRLM 2011** (restructured from **SGSY**), renamed **DAY-NRLM in 2016**; implemented via State Rural Livelihood Missions; Delhi and Chandigarh excluded.
[166:1:2] - Funding **60:40**; **90:10 NE incl. Sikkim**; **UTs 100% Centre** (original NRLM ratio 75:25).
[166:1:3] - **Women SHGs: 10 to 20 members** (5-10 in difficult/hilly areas and for disabled persons).
[166:1:4] - **Mandatory (automatic) inclusion** from SECC: **households without shelter, destitutes living on alms, manual scavengers, PTGs, legally released bonded labourers** – single-parent households are NOT on this list.
[166:1:5] TABLE head: Sub-scheme | Objective
  [166:1:5.0] DDU-GKY (25 Sep 2014) | Placement-linked skilling for poor rural youth (15-35)
  [166:1:5.1] RSETIs | Bank-led self-employment training
  [166:1:5.2] MKSP (Mahila Kisan Sashaktikaran Pariyojana, 2011) | Empower women farmers; about 35.9 lakh women covered (35.92 lakh by Nov 2018; 35.98 lakh by 31 Mar 2019)
  [166:1:5.3] AGEY (Aajeevika Grameen Express Yojana, 2017) | Rural transport run by SHGs to connect remote villages with markets, schools, health centres
  [166:1:5.4] SVEP | Start-up village entrepreneurship
[166:1:6] - **DDU-GKY funding:** **75:25** at launch (2014; NE/Himalayan 90:10) → **60:40** after the 2015-16 CSS rationalisation (NE/Himalayan 90:10; J&K 100% under Himayat).
[166:1:7] - **Lakhpati Didi**: **3 crore women** earning over **₹1 lakh a year**.
[166:1:8] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:166 subs 3, 6; AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 (src/economy/ap-socio-economic-survey-2024-25.txt) p.283 (AGEY under NRLM); svnit.ac.in DDU-GKY orientation deck and manoramayearbook.in (DDU-GKY 60:40, 90:10); etvbharat.com (MKSP 35.97 lakh, Lok Sabha July 2019)

  [166:2:0.10] Agriculture Infrastructure Fund | Post-harvest infrastructure financing | 2020
  [166:2:0.11] PM-PRANAM | Cut chemical fertiliser use to restore the fertility of Mother Earth; States share the subsidy saved | 2023

## SUB 3 | Rural Employment – Lineage, MGNREGA and VB-G RAM G
[166:3:0] TABLE head: Scheme | Year | Note
  [166:3:0.0] IRDP | 1978-79; nationwide 1980 | Self-employment; TRYSEM (15 Aug 1979) and DWCRA (1982-83) were its sub-programmes
  [166:3:0.1] NREP | 1980 (not 1983) | Wage employment
  [166:3:0.2] RLEGP | 1983 | Landless households
  [166:3:0.3] JRY | 1989 | NREP + RLEGP merged
  [166:3:0.4] EAS | 1993 | Employment Assurance Scheme
  [166:3:0.5] SGSY | 1 April 1999 | Merged IRDP, TRYSEM, DWCRA
  [166:3:0.6] SGRY | 2001 | EAS + JGSY
  [166:3:0.7] Bharat Nirman | 2005 | Six rural infrastructure components
  [166:3:0.8] MGNREGA | 2005 | 100 days per household
  [166:3:0.9] VB-G RAM G | Introduced 16 Dec 2025 | Replaces MGNREGA; 125 days
[166:3:1] - **TRYSEM** = Training of Rural Youth for Self-Employment → **employment (self-employment) to rural youths**.
[166:3:2] # MGNREGA
[166:3:3] - **100 days** of **unskilled manual work** per household per year; **one-third** women; unemployment allowance after **15 days**.
[166:3:4] - **Gram Panchayat** plans, executes and implements works on the **Gram Sabha's** recommendation; **Gram Sabha** conducts the **social audit**; AP's **SSAAT** pioneered social audits.
[166:3:5] - **Barefoot technicians**: local youth trained (by NIRDPR) to measure and supervise MGNREGA works.
[166:3:6] - **Geo-MGNREGA (2016):** assets geotagged with ISRO's **Bhuvan**; linked to **SDG 1 and SDG 8**.
[166:3:7] TABLE head: Feature | MGNREGA 2005 | VB-G RAM G
  [166:3:7.0] Full name | Mahatma Gandhi NREGA | Viksit Bharat–Guarantee for Rozgar and Ajeevika Mission (Gramin)
  [166:3:7.1] Guarantee | 100 days | 125 days
  [166:3:7.2] Funding | Centre paid wages | CSS 60:40; NE/Himalayan 90:10
  [166:3:7.3] Allowance | States | Allowance and delay compensation fully by States
  [166:3:7.4] Status | Repealed | Assent 20 Dec 2025; in force 1 July 2026
[166:3:8] - Tagline of VB-G RAM G: **"Rozgar Bhi, Samman Bhi"**.
[166:3:9] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:166 subs 1-2; Laxmikanth (src/polity/laxmikanth-indian-polity-01.txt) p.182 (IRDP 1978, JRY 1989, SGSY 1999, SGRY 2001); NextGen Indian Economy (src/economy/nextgen-indian-economy-appsc-01.txt) p.17 and Rocket Indian Economy (src/economy/rocket-indian-economy.txt) p.51 (TRYSEM rural youth self-employment, NREP, RLEGP)
[166:3:10] - **Bharat Nirman (2005) components:** irrigation, roads, housing, drinking water, electrification, telephony (no skilling).

  x: VB-G RAM G stands for Viksit Bharat-Guarantee for Rozgar and Ajeevika Mission (Gramin).

=== NOTES 2:168 | Andhra Pradesh Welfare and Social Security Programmes
## SUB 0 | Farmer and Occupational Support Schemes
[168:0:0] # YSR Rythu Bharosa (2019-24)
[168:0:1] - **Launched:** **October 2019** (15 Oct, Nellore) – the farmer pillar of **Navaratnalu**; Navaratnalu itself is the whole package, not a farmer scheme.
[168:0:2] - **Amount:** **Rs 13,500 a year per farmer family = Rs 7,500 State + Rs 6,000 PM-KISAN** (three instalments) – SES 2021-22: paid to **land-owning** families and this **includes** the PM-KISAN Rs 6,000 (both statements true).
[168:0:3] - **Promise vs payment:** the 2019 manifesto/Budget promised **Rs 12,500**; raised to **Rs 13,500** at launch – Rs 10,500/14,500/20,500 are distractors.
[168:0:4] - **Tenants:** the State paid the full Rs 13,500 from its own funds to **SC, ST, BC and minority tenant farmers** and **ROFR (forest land) holders**.
[168:0:5] - **Budget 2019-20:** **Rs 8,750 crore** (not 8,570) for **64.06 lakh farmers incl. 15.36 lakh tenant farmers**.
[168:0:6] - **Successor:** **Annadata Sukhibhava** (2024-): **Rs 20,000 = Rs 14,000 State + Rs 6,000 PM-KISAN**, tenants included.
[168:0:7] - **Telangana's Rythu Bharosa** = Rs 12,000 an acre (successor of **Rythu Bandhu**) – do not mix with AP.
[168:0:8] # Rythu Runa Mafi (TDP, 2014)
[168:0:9] - Crop-loan waiver up to **Rs 1.5 lakh per farm family** – the maximum relief.
[168:0:10] # Other Occupational Schemes
[168:0:11] TABLE head: Scheme | Group | Benefit
  [168:0:11.0] YSR Vahana Mitra (2019) | Owner-drivers of autos, taxis, maxi-cabs | Rs 10,000 a year
  [168:0:11.1] Auto Driver Sevalo (2025) | Self-owned auto owners | Rs 15,000 a year (Vahana Mitra's successor)
  [168:0:11.2] Nethanna Nestham | Handloom weavers who own looms | Rs 24,000 a year
  [168:0:11.3] Matsyakara Bharosa | Marine fishermen in the fishing ban | Rs 10,000 → Rs 20,000 per family
  [168:0:11.4] YSR Law Nestham (3 Dec 2019) | Junior advocates | Rs 5,000 a month for 3 years
  [168:0:11.5] Jagananna Chedodu | Self-employed tailors, rajakas, nayee brahmins with shops | Rs 10,000 a year
  [168:0:11.6] Rajiv Yuva Kiranalu (2011) | Youth | PPP job-linking scheme to employ 15 lakh youth
[168:0:12] > Exam angle: Rs 13,500 incl. PM-KISAN (asked 6 times); October 2019; Rs 8,750 crore; Vahana Mitra Rs 10,000.
[168:0:13] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:168 sub 1 (old-notes.txt); NextGen AP Economy STATIC-1 Part 2 p.2 (Yuva Kiranalu); PRS AP Budget 2019-20 analysis and Lokmat Times 12 Jul 2019 (search results)
## SUB 1 | Women, SHG, Marriage and Girl-Child Schemes
[168:1:0] # SHG Institutions
[168:1:1] - **SERP** (Society for Elimination of Rural Poverty) – builds and **leverages community-based organisations** (SHGs, village organisations, mandal samakhyas): **84.47 lakh rural women**; **MEPMA** – **2.74 lakh urban SHGs**.
[168:1:2] - **Streenidhi** – credit cooperative (2011) giving SHG members quick loans; not the CBO-building body.
[168:1:3] - **Anna Sanjeevani** – **generic-medicine shops** run through women's SHG federations (73 Anna Sanjeevani Kendras of 325 generic shops).
[168:1:4] # SHG and Women's Cash Schemes (2019-24)
[168:1:5] TABLE head: Scheme | Benefit
  [168:1:5.0] YSR Aasara | Reimbursed the women SHGs' bank loans outstanding on 11 April 2019 in four instalments; 69,597 members got Rs 633.74 crore (2024-25)
  [168:1:5.1] YSR Cheyutha (12 Aug 2020) | Rs 18,750 a year = Rs 75,000 over 4 years to BC/SC/ST/minority women aged 45-60, AP residents; no Rs 10,000 income condition; beneficiaries identified by the welfare finance corporations with village volunteers
  [168:1:5.2] YSR Kapu Nestham | Kapu, Balija, Telaga, Ontari women aged 45-60 – Rs 15,000 a year
[168:1:6] - The name **Cheyutha** first belonged to a **1998 TDP scheme for handicapped persons**; **Adarana** (1998; Adarana-2 2018) = **modern tools to BC artisans** at subsidised prices through the **BC Corporation**.
[168:1:7] # Marriage Aid
[168:1:8] TABLE head: Scheme | Beneficiary | Amount
  [168:1:8.0] Giri Puthrika Kalyana Pathakam (2015) | Tribal girls at marriage | Rs 50,000 (MeeSeva; District Tribal Welfare Office)
  [168:1:8.1] Dulhan (April 2015) | Brides of poor Muslim families | Rs 50,000
  [168:1:8.2] Chandranna Pelli Kanuka (TDP, 2018) | SC, ST, BC, minorities, disabled | Rs 75,000 for SC inter-caste marriage
  [168:1:8.3] YSR Kalyana Kanuka → YSR Kalyanamasthu / Shaadi Tohfa (2022) | SC, ST and minority brides (Shaadi Tohfa = Muslims) | Rs 1 lakh (BC Rs 50,000)
[168:1:9] - **Minority welfare:** **Imams and Mouzams** get **Rs 5,000-10,000 a month**; pilgrimage aid for **Haj and Jerusalem** (not Haj only).
[168:1:10] # Girl Child, Mother and Child
[168:1:11] - **Bangaru Thalli** (June 2013; renamed **Maa Inti Mahalakshmi**) – for girls born **on or after 1 May 2013** in white-card families → implemented from **1 May 2013**.
[168:1:12] TABLE head: Scheme | Purpose
  [168:1:12.0] Talli Bidda Express | Free drop-back vehicle taking mother and newborn home after delivery
  [168:1:12.1] Giri Gorumudda (2015) | Nutrition for tribal mothers and young children in Agency areas
  [168:1:12.2] YSR Sampoorna Poshana (Plus in tribal mandals) | Richer take-home rations to pregnant/lactating women and children 6-72 months through Anganwadis
[168:1:13] - **55,746 AWCs** in 257 projects; Balasanjeevani SNP reached 4,15,065 pregnant and lactating women.
[168:1:14] > Exam angle: Aasara = loans outstanding on 11 April 2019; Cheyutha = Rs 75,000 over 4 years, women 45-60; Giri Puthrika = tribal girls.
[168:1:15] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:168 sub 5-6 (old-notes.txt); NextGen AP Economy STATIC-1 Part 1 p.13 (Adarana, Cheyutha 1998); NextGen AP Economy STATIC-1 Part 2 p.5-6 (Bangaru Thalli, Dulhan, Giri Gorumuddalu); GKToday on Giriputrika (search result); Deccan Chronicle on generic shops (search result)
## SUB 2 | Education Welfare
[168:2:0] # Amma Vodi → Talliki Vandanam
[168:2:1] - **Jagananna Amma Vodi** (Jan 2020): **Rs 15,000 a year** to the **mother/recognised guardian** of a poor child who **enrols and attends** school – the massive **school-enrolment support** programme (from 2021-22 Rs 2,000 kept for school/toilet maintenance, Rs 13,000 credited).
[168:2:2] - **Talliki Vandanam** (Super Six) replaced it: **Rs 15,000 per child** a year, children in **government or private** schools; aims to raise the **Gross Enrolment Ratio**.
[168:2:3] # Other Education Schemes
[168:2:4] TABLE head: Scheme | What it does
  [168:2:4.0] Jagananna Vidya Deevena | Full fee reimbursement (RTF) for post-matric (beyond Intermediate) students
  [168:2:4.1] Jagananna Vasathi Deevena | Hostel and boarding support
  [168:2:4.2] Mana Badi Nadu-Nedu (14 Nov 2019) | Upgrades government school infrastructure; now Mana Badi – Mana Bhavishyathu
  [168:2:4.3] Jagananna Vidya Kanuka | Student kits; now Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan Vidyarthi Mitra
  [168:2:4.4] Jagananna Gorumudda | Midday meal; now Dokka Seethamma Madhyahana Badi Bojanam
  [168:2:4.5] Badi Pilustondi (2014-15) | Flagship enrolment campaign to bring all children 6-14 to school
  [168:2:4.6] Badikosta | Free bicycles to Class IX girls of government schools – to cut girls' dropout from high school
  [168:2:4.7] Ambedkar Overseas Vidya Nidhi | Aid to SC students for PG/Ph.D. abroad – first in USA, UK, Australia, Canada, Singapore (later more countries)
[168:2:5] > Exam angle: Amma Vodi Rs 15,000 to mothers (asked 5 times); Vidya Deevena = fees; Nadu-Nedu = infrastructure; Badikosta = girls' dropout.
[168:2:6] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:168 sub 7 (old-notes.txt); NextGen AP Economy STATIC-1 Part 2 p.6 (Badi Pilustondi); AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.247 (AOVN); Telangana ePASS overseas page (search result, country list)
## SUB 3 | Delivery Machinery, Women's Safety and Campaigns
[168:3:0] # Delivery Institutions
[168:3:1] TABLE head: Institution | Fact
  [168:3:1.0] e-Seva (2001) | India's first integrated citizen service centres
  [168:3:1.1] Village/Ward Secretariats (2019) | 10,000+ secretariats, 500+ services; now Swarna Gramam / Swarna Ward
  [168:3:1.2] Volunteers (2019) | One per 50 households
  [168:3:1.3] DBT (JAM trinity) | Cuts ghost beneficiaries and middlemen
  [168:3:1.4] PGRS (earlier Spandana) | Grievance redressal
[168:3:2] - **Family is the unit** of welfare delivery (family mapping, Sep 2026).
[168:3:3] # Campaigns
[168:3:4] - **Nava Nirmana Deeksha** – launched **2 June 2015** (first anniversary of bifurcation) to call people to take part in rebuilding the State.
[168:3:5] - **Chandranna Bata** (2014-19) – **internal CC roads in villages**.
[168:3:6] # Zero Poverty – P4
[168:3:7] - Launched **30 March 2025 (Ugadi)**: the **top 10% support the bottom 20%**; phase 1: **21.8 lakh families**; base of **Swarna Andhra @2047** – end extreme poverty by **2030**.
[168:3:8] - Debates: State debt about **36% of GSDP** strains welfare spending.
[168:3:9] # Women's Safety
[168:3:10] - **Disha app** (8 Feb 2020, **Rajamahendravaram**): **SOS** button alerts the **police control room** and shares location; replaced by **Suraksha** (2025). First **Disha police station** – Rajamahendravaram.
[168:3:11] - **AP Disha Bill, 2019**: investigation **7 days** + trial **14 days** = 21 days; amended **IPC and CrPC**; withdrawn as **BNS/BNSS** came into force on **1 July 2024**.
[168:3:12] - **One Stop Centres** (Mission Shakti): police, medical, legal, counselling and shelter under one roof; backed by **Women Helpline 181** (universalised **2019**).
[168:3:13] > Exam angle: Disha = SOS app (asked twice); volunteer per 50 households; Nava Nirmana Deeksha 2 June 2015.
[168:3:14] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:168 sub 4, 9 (old-notes.txt); Nava Nirmana Deeksha and Chandranna Bata: general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 4 | Navaratnalu, Super Six and Renamed Schemes
[168:4:0] # Navaratnalu (YSRCP, 2019-24)
[168:4:1] - Package of **nine** flagship promises, paid from **revenue expenditure**: **Rythu Bharosa**, fee reimbursement, **Aarogyasri**, Jalayagnam, prohibition, **Amma Vodi**, **Pedalandariki Illu**, YSR Aasara/Cheyutha, **Pension Kanuka**.
[168:4:2] - **Not** Navaratnalu: **Vahana Mitra**, Stree Shakti (Super Six).
[168:4:3] - **Pedalandariki Illu** – house sites/houses for **poor women**: **25 lakh homes in five years**; Jagananna colony houses launched **3 June 2021**.
[168:4:4] # Super Six (2024-)
[168:4:5] TABLE head: Guarantee | Benefit
  [168:4:5.0] Talliki Vandanam | Rs 15,000 per school-going child a year
  [168:4:5.1] Stree Shakti | Free bus travel for women in 5 APSRTC categories (not all); launched 15 Aug 2025
  [168:4:5.2] Deepam-2 | 3 free LPG cylinders a year to 83 lakh families (original Deepam, 1999, gave connections)
  [168:4:5.3] Annadata Sukhibhava | Rs 20,000 a year to farmers
  [168:4:5.4] Jobs | 20 lakh jobs or Rs 3,000 unemployment allowance
  [168:4:5.5] Aadabidda Nidhi | Rs 1,500 a month to women
[168:4:6] # Old Name → New Name
[168:4:7] TABLE head: Old (YSRCP) | New
  [168:4:7.0] Rythu Bharosa | Annadata Sukhibhava
  [168:4:7.1] Jagananna Amma Vodi | Talliki Vandanam
  [168:4:7.2] YSR Pension Kanuka | NTR Bharosa
  [168:4:7.3] YSR Vahana Mitra | Auto Driver Sevalo
  [168:4:7.4] Dr YSR Aarogyasri | Dr NTR Vaidya Seva
  [168:4:7.5] Spandana | PGRS
  [168:4:7.6] Jagananna Vidya Kanuka | Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan Vidyarthi Mitra
[168:4:8] > Exam angle: "not a Navaratnalu pillar" → Vahana Mitra; Rythu Bharosa = farmer pillar.

## SUB 5 | Health Welfare
[168:5:0] # Aarogyasri → Dr NTR Vaidya Seva
[168:5:1] TABLE head: Year | Name
  [168:5:1.0] 1 April 2007 | Rajiv Aarogyasri – named after Rajiv Gandhi, under Y.S. Rajasekhara Reddy
  [168:5:1.1] Nov 2014 | NTR Vaidya Seva
  [168:5:1.2] 2019 | Dr YSR Aarogyasri
  [168:5:1.3] 2024 | Dr NTR Vaidya Seva
[168:5:2] - **YSR Aarogyasri (2019-24):** families with income **below Rs 5 lakh**; any treatment costing **Rs 1,000 or more**; empanelled hospitals in **Hyderabad, Chennai, Bengaluru** (not the whole country); **Rs 10,000 a month pension** for kidney, thalassaemia and similar patients.
[168:5:3] - **Insurance model (May 2025, Health Minister Y. Satya Kumar Yadav):** cover up to **Rs 25 lakh per family a year** for **1.65 crore poor and middle-class** families (not only BPL); every citizen **Rs 2.5 lakh** (Universal Health Policy).
[168:5:4] - Cashless care for **3,257 procedures**; **EHS** for State employees and pensioners.
[168:5:5] TABLE head: Service | Feature
  [168:5:5.0] 108 | Emergency ambulance
  [168:5:5.1] 104 | Mobile medical units, fixed-day village visits
  [168:5:5.2] Family Physician | Launched 6 April 2023 at Lingamguntla (Chilakaluripet)
[168:5:6] - **MMR 45, IMR 24** (2024-25).
[168:5:7] > Exam angle: Aarogyasri named after Rajiv Gandhi; Rs 25 lakh cover (2025); income limit Rs 5 lakh, Rs 1,000 threshold.
[168:5:8] ~~ Sources: NextGen AP Economy STATIC-1 Part 2 p.1, p.6; AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.225; app notes 2:168 sub 3 (old-notes.txt); The South First on revamped YSR Aarogyasri (search result)
## SUB 6 | Social Security Pensions
[168:6:0] # Names and Amounts
[168:6:1] TABLE head: Period | Name | Old-age / widow pension
  [168:6:1.0] 2014-19 | NTR Bharosa | Rs 1,000, later Rs 2,000
  [168:6:1.1] 2019-24 | YSR Pension Kanuka | In steps to Rs 3,000 (Jan 2022: Rs 2,250 → Rs 2,500)
  [168:6:1.2] June 2024- | NTR Bharosa | Rs 4,000 (from Rs 3,000, April 2024)
[168:6:2] - **NTR Bharosa** = AP's **social security pension scheme**; **3.5 lakh new pensions** sanctioned in **January 2017**.
[168:6:3] TABLE head: Category | Per month
  [168:6:3.0] Old age (60+), widows (18+) | Rs 4,000
  [168:6:3.1] Disabled (40%+) | Rs 6,000
  [168:6:3.2] Thalassaemia and similar | Rs 10,000
[168:6:4] - **61.92 lakh** beneficiaries; Budget 2026-27 **Rs 27,719 crore**; doorstep delivery with **Aadhaar biometric** authentication; nodal agency **SERP**.
[168:6:5] - AP **tops up the central NSAP** pension; **16th Finance Commission**: highest **per-capita social pension spending** among States.
[168:6:6] - Other States: **Mukhyamantri Vriddhajan Pension Yojana = Bihar** (poor above 60).
[168:6:7] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:168 sub 2 (old-notes.txt); Testbook item on Jan 2017 pensions (search result)
## SUB 7 | Food, Housing and Sanitation
[168:7:0] - **Door delivery of ration** by mobile dispensing units – **2021**; proposal: **5 kg rice per family member**, the family as the unit.
[168:7:1] - **Anna Canteens**: meals at **Rs 5** for the **urban poor**; 62 rural canteens opened at **Dharanikota (Palnadu)**, 15 Apr 2026.
[168:7:2] - **AP State Housing Corporation**: Weaker Section Housing Programme – **"Housing for All"**; current policy: house sites of **3 cents rural / 2 cents urban**.
[168:7:3] - **CLAP** (Clean Andhra Pradesh): **door-to-door segregated garbage** collection, launched **2 Oct 2021**; **Swachh Andhra** is one of the **10 Sutralu**.
[168:7:4] - **Jal Jeevan Mission**: tap water to all rural households by **2027**.
[168:7:5] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:168 sub 8 (old-notes.txt)

=== NOTES 2:169 | Andhra Pradesh Policies and Programmes – Agriculture, Industry, It and Infrastructure
## SUB 0 | E-governance and Real Time Governance
[169:0:0] # Real Time Governance (RTGS)
[169:0:1] - **RTGS**: e-governance tools for **maximum governance with minimum government**, with **real-time performance indicators**; command centre at the **Secretariat** watching services and complaints live – the initiative **first launched in Andhra Pradesh**.
[169:0:2] - Motto: **"People First"** (People First 1100 call centre takes citizens' feedback).
[169:0:3] - Uses the **Hitachi Visualization Suite** – disaster plans and other real-time data on a **single pane of glass** for timely decisions.
[169:0:4] - **CORE dashboard** = **CM Office Real-time Executive** dashboard tracking departments' indicators.
[169:0:5] # Other Platforms
[169:0:6] TABLE head: Initiative | Purpose
  [169:0:6.0] e-Seva (2001) | India's first integrated citizen service centres
  [169:0:6.1] MeeSeva | Single-window delivery of certificates and services
  [169:0:6.2] BhuSeva | Integrated land-records platform; Bhudhaar number per land parcel
  [169:0:6.3] AP FiberNet (2017) | Low-cost broadband, TV and telephone to homes and offices
  [169:0:6.4] Jnanabhumi (2017) | Common paperless platform for all scholarships and education-related schemes
  [169:0:6.5] Pedarikampai Gelupu (PPG) | Common online platform of the welfare (finance) corporations, linked to federations and bank branches – not scholarships
[169:0:7] > Exam angle: RTGS "first in AP", "People First", Hitachi; scholarships = Jnanabhumi vs welfare corporations = PPG.
[169:0:8] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:169 sub 4 (old-notes.txt); Testbook item on Pedarikampai Gelupu (fetched); Hitachi, CORE, People First: general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 1 | Agriculture and Groundwater Programmes
[169:1:0] # Programmes
[169:1:1] TABLE head: Programme | Year | Purpose / benefit
  [169:1:1.0] Krishi Cabinet | June 2015 | Cabinet sub-committee steering the Primary Sector Mission
  [169:1:1.1] APCNF | Since 2016 | Natural farming run by Rythu Sadhikara Samstha (RySS)
  [169:1:1.2] Rythu Bharosa Kendras (RBKs) | 2020 | Village one-stop centres for seeds, fertilisers, advisory, procurement
  [169:1:1.3] YSR Jala Kala | 2020 | Free borewells for small and marginal farmers – single or group farmers with 2.5 acres; a policy against irrigation water shortage
  [169:1:1.4] YSR Zero Interest Crop Loans | 20 April 2021 | Crop loans up to Rs 1 lakh repaid on time become interest-free
  [169:1:1.5] e-Crop | – | Digital booking of the crop on every field each season
[169:1:2] - **CCRC** (AP Crop Cultivators Rights Act, 2019): tenants get access to **loans, insurance and input subsidy**; **no ownership title**.
[169:1:3] - **PMFBY:** farmer premium **2% kharif, 1.5% rabi**, 5% commercial; **voluntary for all since Kharif 2020**.
[169:1:4] # APFAMGS (Farmer Managed Groundwater Systems)
[169:1:5] - **FAO**-supported (2004-09), 7 drought-prone districts: farmers measure and manage groundwater **demand** themselves – **demand-side** management.
[169:1:6] - Principle: **rules are set by the users** (farmer groups), not by outsiders.
[169:1:7] - **AP Integrated Irrigation and Agriculture Transformation Project** – **World Bank** (USD 172.2 million, 2018).
[169:1:8] > Exam angle: Jala Kala = free borewells (asked twice); APFAMGS demand side, user-made rules.
[169:1:9] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:169 sub 0, 7 (old-notes.txt); CARE APPSC 2026-04 (src/current-affairs/care-appsc-2026-04.txt, YSR Jala Kala); Krishi Cabinet: general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 2 | Swarna Andhra @2047 and Zero Poverty – P4
[169:2:0] - Vision unveiled **December 2024**; frame for the **2024-29 policies** and industrial corridors.
[169:2:1] - 2047 targets: GSDP **USD 2.4 trillion**; per capita income **about USD 42,000**; **net zero by 2047**.
[169:2:2] - **Ten principles (Sutralu)** – the first is **zero poverty**.
[169:2:3] - **Zero Poverty – P4 Policy** (**P**ublic-**P**rivate-**P**eople **P**artnership): launched **30 March 2025 (Ugadi)**, the cornerstone of Swarna Andhra @2047 – aims to **eradicate poverty** (zero poverty), not investment or tourism.
[169:2:4] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:169 sub 1 and 2:168 sub 9 (old-notes.txt)
## SUB 3 | Infrastructure, Ports and Amaravati
[169:3:0] # Amaravati
[169:3:1] - **2 June 2014** – bifurcation; **APCRDA Act, 2014**; foundation stone **22 October 2015** by **PM Narendra Modi**.
[169:3:2] - **2017**: AP government and a **Singapore consortium** set up the partnership for the **Amaravati Capital City Start-Up Area** (not 2014).
[169:3:3] - Phase I: **World Bank USD 800 million + ADB** (Dec 2024).

  [169:3:5.0] Duggarajapatnam port | Vakadu mandal – SPSR Nellore district when asked; Tirupati district since 2022
  [169:3:5.1] Machilipatnam port | Krishna district; limits cut 64 → 15 km under the Indian Ports Act, 2025
  [169:3:5.2] Ramayapatnam port | Limits cut 51 → 15 km
  [169:3:5.3] Mulapeta port | Srikakulam
  [169:3:5.4] Bhogapuram airport (Alluri Sitarama Raju International) | Vizianagaram; GMR, PPP
  [169:3:5.5] Polavaram | Godavari; national project under Section 90, AP Reorganisation Act 2014; target 2027

## SUB 4 | Industrial, IT and Startup Policies 4.0

  [169:4:1.0] Industrial Development 4.0 | G.O.Ms.68 | Investment target Rs 30 lakh crore
  [169:4:1.1] MSME & Entrepreneur Development 4.0 | G.O.Ms.69 | "One Family, One Entrepreneur"
  [169:4:1.2] Electronics Manufacturing 4.0 | G.O.Ms.70 | USD 50 bn production, USD 10 bn investment, 5 lakh jobs
  [169:4:1.3] Food Processing 4.0 | G.O.Ms.71 | Agri value addition
[169:4:2] - **Innovation and Startup Policy (4.0) 2024-29**: issued **April 2025** by the **ITE&C (Promotions) Department** – not Industries & Commerce.
[169:4:3] - **IT & GCC Policy 4.0**: **12 Dec 2024**; **LIFT** = **Land Incentive for Tech Hubs**; **Data Center Policy 4.0**: **November 2024**; **Google** USD 15 bn AI hub at **Visakhapatnam** (Oct 2025).
[169:4:4] - **Large project** = fixed capital **above Rs 501 crore up to Rs 1,000 crore**; **Mega project** = **2,000+ direct jobs**.
[169:4:5] # Agencies and Hubs
[169:4:6] - **APIIC** – nodal agency for plug-and-play industrial parks; allots up to **50 acres** without Cabinet approval (June 2026). **SIPB** chaired by the **Chief Minister**; **Single Desk Portal** – single-window clearances.
[169:4:7] - **BRAP**: AP 1st in **2016 (jointly with Telangana), 2017 and 2019**.

  [169:4:8.4] Bulk Drug Park | Nakkapalli (Anakapalli)

## SUB 5 | Energy, Green and Aqua Policies
[169:5:0] - **Tribal mini hydel plants:** APTRIPCO built **1.6 MW** plants at Pinjarikonda, Metlapalem and **Vetamamidi**; **Vetamamidi** (Addateegala mandal) began production in **2011**.
[169:5:1] - **AP Integrated Clean Energy Policy, 2024**: solar, wind, hybrid, **pumped storage**, **green hydrogen/ammonia**; target **160 GW**; **net zero by 2047** (not 2070).
[169:5:2] - **Pinnapuram (Kurnool)**: Greenko integrated solar-wind-**pumped storage** project.
[169:5:3] - **NTPC Green Hydrogen Hub, Pudimadaka** (Anakapalli): JV of NTPC Green Energy and **NREDCAP**.
[169:5:4] - **AP State Aquaculture Development Authority Act, 2020**: regulates **aqua farms, seed and feed**.
[169:5:5] - Fishing harbours Phase I: **Juvvaladinne, Nizampatnam, Machilipatnam, Uppada** (Pudimadaka is Phase II).
[169:5:6] ~~ Sources: app notes 2:169 sub 3 (old-notes.txt); Hans India 24 Apr 2017 on mini hydel projects (search result)
